James Talarico becomes first Democrat to win Texas Senate seat since 1988

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Americans are electing members of Congress, which is made up of the House of Representatives and the Senate. All 435 House seats are up for election. In the Senate, only 35 of 100 seats are being contested because senators serve six-year terms. This results tracker shows both the races being decided now and the 65 seats that are not up for election this year - 34 held by Democrats and 31 by Republicans.

Democrat James Talarico has won a key Senate race in Texas, in what is likely the single biggest upset for Republicans in this year's elections.

Talarico's stunning victory over Republican Ken Paxton marks the first time a Democrat has won a US Senate seat in Texas since 1988 - and has helped solidify Democrats taking full control of the US Congress in a tidal wave of wins across America.

Talarico, a former schoolteacher, was a little-known figure until last year. He made the rising cost of living under Republican leadership a central theme of his run.

Paxton sought to portray Talarico's views as too progressive for the state, and campaigned alongside Trump on several occasions.

FOLLOW LIVE: Election results and analysis of the US midterms

LIVE RESULTS: Follow live results as they come into the BBC

ANALYSIS: Democrats eye sweeping gains and reshaping of political map

Supporters of Talarico went berserk as TVs at a watch party in Austin, the state's capitol, projected him the winner.

They hugged, they kissed, and they drank to a reality that many say they dared not believe would come true.

Emerging on stage to rapturous applause at his official watch party at a nearby performing arts theatre, Talarico pronounced: "We just took back Texas."

"We just proved that all the money in the world is no match for the people of Texas," he continued, referring to his win over Paxton whose campaign was backed by Elon Musk, the world's richest man.

He invoked religious themes that have grown familiar to his campaign, comparing his unlikely odds to the biblical story of David and Goliath.

"This win, this win is for every Texan who's ever wondered if anyone is listening," he said. "I hear you, we hear you. And tonight the world hears you."

For decades, the idea of a Democrat winning a statewide race had felt like a pipe dream for Texas liberals.

As the nation's second most populous state, Texas has been a bastion of conservatism, routinely rolling out Republican policies that are later employed elsewhere.

But throughout the campaign, polls suggested state legislator Talarico was breaking through with voters, energising a Texas Democratic base desperate for new leadership.

Spending in the race skyrocketed, and it quickly grew to become the most expensive US Senate race in history, with more than half a billion dollars spent.

Billionaire Elon Musk poured in $158m (Â£117m) to a super PAC supporting Paxton's campaign - a point that Talarico used in attacks on Paxton's campaign.

Then two members of the Bush family, the Republican political dynasty that counts Texas as its political launchpad, backed Talarico in a stunning move foreshadowing Paxton's doom.

George Washington University political science professor Quardricos Driskell told the BBC that Paxton was a major part of Republicans' downfall, calling him an "objectively terrible candidate".

He said the win in Texas by Talarico, combined with another upset on Tuesday that ousted the state's Republican governor Greg Abbott, was "absolutely historic". Abbott's loss marked the first time a Democrat had won a race for governor in the state since the 1990s.

"This wasn't just a tsunami, not just a referendum, but this was a bloodbath," Driskell said about Democrats gains in the Lone Star State. "A clear distinction that the country wants to go in a different direction".

Talarico defeated a well-known opponent, Dallas Congresswoman Jasmine Crockett, in the Democratic primary. Then continued to maintain good polling numbers despite an onslaught of negative adverts, including attacks on past comments where he supported transgender people.

Throughout his campaign, he frequently leaned into his religious upbringing â as the grandson of a Southern Baptist preacher who spent time in a seminary â sprinkling his stump speeches with biblical references.

Paxton had been beleaguered by a number of political scandals, including his impeachment by his own party in 2023 on corruption accusations, before he was later acquitted by the state's Senate.

He was also indicted by the US Securities and Exchange Commission on federal fraud charges, but they were later dismissed.

Trump made multiple stops in Texas to beef up support for Paxton and other Republicans - including in reliably-Republican spots, signalling how concerned party leadership was about turnout.

Voters have been frustrated with high consumer costs, including the price of petrol, and largely blame Trump's tariff policies and the Iran war.

Hispanic voters and those who live along the southern US border have also taken issue with Trump's immigration policies.

Despite border crossings plummeting under Trump - something that had been popular with Texas voters - polls suggested Trump's support among Hispanics, who make up a third of the state's population, had ebbed significantly.

Surveys taken since late summer indicated a majority of Texan Hispanic voters - many of whom switched to Trump to help him win the White House in 2024 - now disapproved of the president. They also suggested that Hispanic voters were favouring Democrats over Republicans by a significant margin.

Many Texas Hispanics who spoke to the BBC before the election pointed to Trump's immigration crackdown as the reasoning behind their change of heart. They pointed to aggressive measures that led to citizens being detained and non-violent offenders being rounded up indiscriminately.

South Carolina elects Democratic senator for first time in almost three decades

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Americans are electing members of Congress, which is made up of the House of Representatives and the Senate. All 435 House seats are up for election. In the Senate, only 35 of 100 seats are being contested because senators serve six-year terms. This results tracker shows both the races being decided now and the 65 seats that are not up for election this year - 34 held by Democrats and 31 by Republicans.

Watch: Key moments from the US midterm election results

Republican South Carolina Senate candidate Darline Graham is projected to have lost her race, handing Democrats their first victory in the state for 28 years.

Democrat Annie Andrews, a former paediatrician, was on course to pull off a stunning upset in Tuesday's midterm elections, according to the BBC's US partner CBS.

Graham was appointed to fill the seat in July after the sudden death of her brother, Senator Lindsey Graham, a move that won support from President Donald Trump.

A political newcomer, she conceded during the primary that she was "not that informed on national security".

South Carolina Senator Darline Graham loses seat she filled after her brother former-Senator Lindsay Graham died in July

FOLLOW LIVE: Election results and analysis of the US midterms

LIVE RESULTS: Follow live results as they come into the BBC

ANALYSIS: Democrats eye sweeping gains and reshaping of political map

Trump won the state by 18 points in 2024.

During her primary campaign, Graham acknowledged her lack of political experience, particularly after stumbling on a foreign policy question during a recent debate.

Asked about the US stance towards Taiwan, Graham had said: "National security is not my thing, not my area of expertise, but I do support the military."

Her brother, Lindsey Graham, was elected to the Senate in 2002 and was one of Washington's most influential voices on foreign policy, often pushing for US military intervention overseas.

Transfer news LIVE: Arsenal triple raid; Man Utd want Bellingham; Chelsea, Liverpool latest

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Arsenal, Chelsea, Manchester United, Liverpool and Tottenham are stepping up their preparations for the January transfer window, with less than two months until it opens. Arsenal are expected to prioritise a new striker after Viktor Gyokeres and Kai Havertz struggled for consistency. Bournemouth forward Eli Junior Kroupi is among their leading targets, while Real Madrid’s Endrick and Atletico Madrid’s Julian Alvarez have also been linked with moves to north London.

Manchester United are keeping faith with Michael Carrick for now, but his future remains uncertain ahead of fixtures against Roma and Aston Villa. The club are expected to strengthen regardless, with former Brentford striker Ivan Toney linked with a move to Old Trafford alongside Everton defender Jarrad Branthwaite. Chelsea, meanwhile, are reportedly making progress towards signing Bournemouth midfielder Alex Scott, while their interest in Lamine Camara has cooled, potentially leaving Liverpool in contention for the Monaco star.

Tottenham are also monitoring Scott and Kroupi as they assess their January options. A new striker is expected to feature prominently on their recruitment agenda, with Spurs keen to strengthen their squad. Several Premier League rivals are already identifying targets ahead of what could be a busy winter window. Follow all the latest transfer news, rumours and gossip live below!

Michael Carrick has eased pressure on his job with Manchester United ‘s come-from-behind win over Roma, but the threat of the sack remains alive.

Carrick will now hope his team have momentum ahead of a crucial clash with Aston Villa on Sunday before the international break. The head coach will now that he is not yet out of the woods.

United's hierarchy held crisis talks over the head coach's position after a 3-0 defeat home defeat to the hands Bournemouth earlier in October.

However, United opted against making an immediate change last month, allowing Carrick to remain in charge for defeat at Chelsea and Tuesday’s late triumph over Roma.

According to The Athletic, Man Utd chiefs last week agreed to keep Carrick in post for the visit of Roma and Sunday’s visit of Aston Villa in the Premier League before reassessing the situation during the break.

That stance is not expected to change despite a much-needed victory.

Man Utd chiefs are said to be happy for Carrick to remain in charge until the international break, at which point a new manager could be appointment and given extra time to settle in the job before domestic football return.

Bayern Munich are keeping tabs on Arsenal captain Martin Odegaard amid uncertainty over his next contract.

Caught Offside claim that the German champions have reportedly maintained contact with Odegaard’s representatives ahead of a possible swoop.

Barcelona are considering a move for Arsenal defender Piero Hincapie, with sporting director Deco including the 24-year-old on his list of centre-back targets.

According to Sport, the Ecuador international’s versatility has attracted interest, although Arsenal are reluctant to lose him during the season.

Meanwhile, Martin Zubimendi is reportedly prepared to consider leaving the Emirates in January after struggling for regular minutes.

TEAMtalk claims several European clubs, including Real Madrid, Barcelona and Bayern, have been made aware of his situation.

Sunderland have announced that Brian Brobbey has signed a new long-term contract.

Pascal De Maesschalck has admitted he was surprised to be appointed Arsenal's academy director.

The Belgian began work at Arsenal in the summer, arriving from Strasbourg where he had been technical director since 2024.

De Maesschalck oversees the Gunners' academy programme, taking over from Per Mertesacker after he left at the end of last season having been in the role for eight years.

In an interview with Sporza, De Maesschalck revealed he viewed the interview process with Arsenal as a good opportunity in itself, before he ended up then getting the job.

Scott Parker is set to succeed Nuno Espirito Santo as West Ham head coach.

Nuno was relieved of his duties with immediate effect on Tuesday morning following back-to-back home defeats to Southampton and West Brom.

The Irons are four points off the automatic promotion places and have won six of their opening 13 Championship games this season.

West Ham invested heavily over the summer, breaking the Championship transfer record to sign midfielder Arne Engels from Celtic for £22million, and were among the favourites for promotion back to the Premier League.

Now, though, after a mixed start to the season, they have decided to part ways with Nuno and have landed on club legend Parker to take the reins as they look to get their promotion bid back on track.

Well, West Ham are not wasting any time in finding a replacement for Nuno Espirito Santo.

The Hammers announced this morning that they had parted company with Nuno and now the Athletic claim an agreement has been struck with former midfielder with Scott Parker.

There is hope Parker will be in appointed in time to take charge for Wednesday’s match against Cardiff.

Chelsea and Liverpool have both made approaches to sign Brentford left-back El Hadji Malick Diouf ahead of next summer’s transfer window.

The 21-year-old Senegal international only joined the Bees towards the end of the summer window but has already attracted interest from two Premier League rivals.

According to D Sports, both clubs have contacted Diouf’s agent to register their interest. The defender is under contract until 2031.

No decision has been made over his future, but a potential departure could develop sooner than expected.

West Ham have sacked Nuno Espirito Santo as head coach.

A statement reads: “West Ham United can confirm that men’s first-team Head Coach Nuno Espirito Santo has left his position with immediate effect.

“Following a thorough review of the start to the EFL Championship season, the Board of Directors believe that a change is necessary at this time in order to ensure that the Club’s objectives and expectations remain on track.

“Assistant Head Coach Rui Pedro Silva and Goalkeeper Coach Rui Barbosa have also departed.

“West Ham United would like to thank Nuno, Rui and Rui for their hard work and commitment during their time at the Club, and wish them all the very best for the future.

“The process of recruiting a new Head Coach is already underway.”

Atletico Madrid have reportedly reached an agreement in principle on personal terms with Manchester United defender Lisandro Martinez.

The 28-year-old is said to be keen on joining Diego Simeone’s side, with his current contract due to expire in 2027.

According to North Bank Insider, several meetings have taken place in Madrid and discussions between the player's representatives and Atletico are advanced.

However, the Spanish club have yet to make official contact with United. A January move or a departure next summer remain possible.

Manchester United sent scouts to watch four Borussia Dortmund players during Saturday’s 2-2 draw with Bayern Munich as they assess potential transfer targets.

Serhou Guirassy, Jobe Bellingham, Felix Nmecha and Daniel Svensson are all reportedly on their radar.

According to TEAMtalk, Guirassy, Bellingham and Nmecha have been monitored throughout the season, although deals for the trio would be difficult due to their high valuations.

Svensson has also emerged as an option to strengthen United’s left-back position, but a move for the 24-year-old is considered more likely next summer than in January.

Tributes pour in for Tenerife striker Jesus de Miguel as Raphinha sends 'message of strength' to family

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Fans laid flowers outside Tenerife’s stadium while football figures across the world expressed their condolences

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Tributes have flooded in for Tenerife striker Jesus de Miguel, who died on Tuesday from injuries sustained in an on-field collision.

The 30-year-old suffered a cardiac arrest in a clash with Andorra goalkeeper Jesus Owono in a Spanish second-tier match on Monday and was rushed to hospital, with the match subsequently abandoned. Although Tenerife later released a statement saying he was stable, his condition worsened in the early hours of Tuesday and he died at 8am.

Fans have congregated outside Tenerife’s home stadium, the Estadio Heliodoro Rodriguez Lopez, to lay flowers and light candles below a picture of the late player, and a farewell service will be held on Saturday at the ground which will be open to supporters.

The club announced six days of official mourning during which all club activities are suspended, while players, staff and officials will attend his funeral.

A minute’s silence was held before each of Tuesday night’s Champions League fixtures and will take place before all other Uefa fixtures this week.

Barcelona’s Raphinha, who scored twice during their Champions League win over Aston Villa, said afterwards: “Today I don't want to talk about football, I want to send a message to the family of Jesus - may he rest in peace.

“From the club, I want to send a message of strength to the family.”

Fellow Spaniard Arsenal boss Mikel Arteta addressed the “heartbreaking” death in a press conference on Tuesday, saying: “To see a colleague lose his life in such a manner, it's beyond any words that I can put today”.

Real Madrid manager Jose Mourinho said: “I think it's something very shocking for the world in general, so imagine for the world of football, for people like us who are on the pitch competing every day.”

He also expressed his concern for Owono, who was given a straight red card after the collision and who has since deactivated his Instagram account. Mourinho said it was a “very, very, very difficult moment” for the Andorra keeper. Spanish police have opened a “routine” investigation into possible involuntary manslaughter; under national law any death which is deemed non-natural automatically triggers an investigation.

The Spanish Football Federation announced the postponement of Tuesday’s Copa del Rey and Copa del Reina matches in honour of De Miguel.

“We extend our deepest condolences to his family, friends and loved ones, as well as to Club Deportivo Tenerife and all its fans, whom we accompany in these moments of great sorrow,” it said.

De Miguel made his professional debut in 2015 for Leganes B and played for several Spanish clubs before joining Tenerife from Castellon in 2025. He signed a three-year contract with Tenerife last summer and helped the club earn promotion back to the Segunda Division, scoring 11 goals in 29 games.

QPR will be on red alert as Bradford City worry emerges

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

QPR went into the 2026/27 campaign hoping they could climb the Championship table and get into the mix for a top eight finish.

And it’s been a positive start for Julien Stéphan’s side, who finished 15th in the previous term, with the team showing promising form in these opening months.

The London outfit haven’t competed in the top flight since 2015, and have not earned a play-off place in the years since, but a busy summer transfer window led to fans being optimistic about the year ahead.

However, it also meant that someone like Kieran Morgan was pushed out of the side, with the 20-year-old going out on loan to League One side Bradford City.

Graham Alexander was in charge when Morgan made the switch to the Bantams in the final days of the summer market.

But he has since become the Preston North End head coach, with Michael Duff stepping in as his replacement.

And Morgan has struggled for game time under both managers, playing just twice before the change in the dugout.

He’s made a total of three appearances in League One this season, all of which came as a substitute, for a total of just 44 minutes of game time.

Morgan was earning regular game time under Stéphan last season, with the midfielder featuring 29 times in the Championship, including 16 starts.

He even contributed two goals and one assist, but it was deemed that he needed a loan spell in League One in order to receive more consistent minutes.

Charlie Austin has criticised coaches for preying on gullible parents on social media

But this hasn’t been the case after two months with Bradford, and the London outfit will not be pleased with his lack of progress.

It would not be surprising if the Hoops acted in the January window and opted to take Morgan out of Valley Parade in order to get his campaign back on track.

If Morgan is unable to break into Duff’s plans between now and 1 January, then other League One clubs could have an opportunity to strike a deal with QPR in the new year.

The likes of Luton Town, Plymouth Argyle, Sheffield Wednesday and Leicester City could all turn their attention to his services in the winter market.

Should QPR recall Kieran Morgan in January? Comment your opinion below

Morgan hasn’t been able to impress Duff since he arrived at Bradford, but he is still only 20-years-of-age, and has decent Championship experience under his belt already.

In the right environment, he has the potential to make a positive impact at this level, having achieved an 81.0 per cent pass accuracy in the second tier last season, completing an impressive 6.62 line-breaking passes in that period (all stats from Fotmob).

This is also a crucial season for Morgan’s own development at this level, as he is at risk of falling completely out of favour at Loftus Road if he can’t improve as a player.

He’s earned 59 appearances in the Championship in his career already, and it’s clear that QPR see something promising in him.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

But if the midfielder can’t prove himself in League One, whether with Bradford or elsewhere, then it will be hard to justify bringing him back into the fold in Stéphan’s side next year.

It is crucial for Morgan’s development that he either convinces Duff to give him a chance, or to find a better temporary home for the second half of the season.

West Ham’s big guns come out with verdict over Scott Parker appointment

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Whilst officially West Ham United head into the midweek fixture against Cardiff City without a head coach, the Irons will mix the talents of established coaching duo Gerard Prenderville and Steve Potts with the expertise of sporting director Mark Noble to provide the off-field leadership for tonight’s attempt to arrest their two-game losing run and put some points on the board.

It will be intriguing to see the immediate impact which the sacking of Nuno Epirito Santo has on the Hammers’ dressing room when the whistle blows tonight.

Hopefully that means the end of ‘outcast’ squad members and that players will get utilised within their established positions soon enough, so that the era of square pegs being pushed into round holes is finally ended.

Many will feel relieved that the days of the division of the Hammers’ squad are ended: Now perhaps the handbrake could come off for the careers of several ‘outcasts’.

Behind the scenes the appointment of Scott Parker does seem nailed on, whilst no official pronouncement has yet been made. All roads point to the former Hammer of the Year taking control of the West Ham squad, which is an appointment that has already been welcomed by former players – one a former assistant to David Moyes who is, of course, still pretty close to the club.

Kevin Nolan, former captain during West Ham’s last promotion winning campaign, pronounced judgement on TalkSPORT:

“I’m delighted with the talk that it’s Scotty going in, giving a young British manager an opportunity at a big club. He’s got a track record. It’s going to be a tough season”

Former Irons’ striker turned TV pundit Dean Ashton was similarly positive. Speaking on talksport, Ashton pointed out that West Ham supporters hadn’t had a manager who felt connected with them for many years, but all that would change if and when Parker is confirmed:

“They want to be inspired again. It is important to connect again with the supporters… He’s certainly got fire in his belly and will relish the opportunity to manage West Ham.”

Whilst some fans profess disappointment over the ‘low ambition’ shown by the football club in considering Scott Parker, I’d suggest they’ve picked exactly the right man to do the job.

Forget the long term. Climb the greasy pole out of this hugely challenging division at all costs and back in the Premier League first. Then worry about what kind of football you want to play.

Because failure carries unthinkable financial repercussions for this football club. Right now, I’d suspect on track records alone, there’s nobody better qualified than former West Ham captain Scott Parker to do the job required.

From the old Bobby Moore Upper to the Billy Bonds' stand these days - I've been watching since '03 and a supporter since about 1970.. Favourite player - Dean Ashton: Curse you Shaun Wright-Phillips. I still watch YouTube repeats of the Cup Final of 2006 hoping in vain that Shaka Hislop grows another six inches and stops Steven Gerrard's injury time equaliser. Can tell I'm getting old knowing I watched both Mark Noble's debut and his last game at West Ham. Pulling on a Claret and Blue replica shirt still makes me feel the same butterflies as when I was seven years old. Magic.

L'Inter e l'insegnamento arrivato nel pre partita. Ecco chi ha inciso di più. Un segnale che...

"Un derby perso non cancella tutto ciò che abbiamo fatto, c’è ancora molta strada da fare. Abbiamo dimostrato di essere squadra e di poter reagire". In conferenza stampa post Feyenoord, Cristian Chivu è tornato per un attimo sul derby per poi dare uno sguardo un po' più ampio alla stagione nerazzurra dove l'approccio alle gare era ed è uno dei grandi temi. Non con gli olandesi dove la squadra è entrata in campo subito concentrata e determinata.

"Seconda vittoria di fila dopo quella con lo Shakhtar. Gli ottavi diretti, ovviamente ancora difficili visto il calendario, non sono più un miraggio. E infatti non lo è stato", sottolinea la Gazzetta dello Sport.

"Soprattutto dopo un derby perso 3-1 e una sfilza di critiche legate all’approccio, il tallone d’Achille che nell’ultimo periodo s’era infiammato più e più volte, costringendo l’Inter ad aumentare il ritmo. Stavolta ne è uscita una vittoria tranquilla e senza rincorrere: un 3-0 facile facile confezionato da Bisseck, Pio e Zielinski. A incidere di più sono stati soprattutto i giocatori che avevano deluso nel derby - Barella, Spence, Carlos Augusto... -, un altro segnale di come i confronti negli stanzoni segreti di Appiano abbiano dato i loro frutti".

Why It Took Years For Boeing To Finally Get The Orders For The 787-10 It Always Wanted

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Although the Boeing 787 Dreamliner made history by becoming the best-selling widebody jet of all time, the largest variant in the lineup has only recently begun to hit the sales figures its maker hoped for over a decade ago. Boeing launched its largest Dreamliner variant at the 2013 Paris Air Show, but by mid-2025, the 787-10 accounted for just 433 gross orders. That is in comparison to the 1,557 orders for the 787-9.

This slow start mirrored an industry consensus that the aircraft lacked the necessary range to compete effectively on highly demanding transoceanic routes, leaving the variant relegated to high-density regional or shorter transatlantic corridors. The sales trajectory of the platform shifted dramatically between late 2025 and early 2026 amid a wave of high-profile commitments. Major international network operators, including Qatar Airways, British Airways, Korean Air, and Turkish Airlines, all placed substantial firm orders for the largest Dreamliner variant.

Delta Air Lines even broke a decade of exclusive Airbus widebody loyalty by announcing a landmark firm order for 30 of the aircraft in January 2026. Concurrently, United Airlines executed a major strategic pivot, converting 56 of its outstanding 787-9 orders to the larger 787-10 model. This sudden surge raises a fundamental question regarding whether commercial airlines shifted their underlying network range requirements, or if Boeing introduced critical hardware enhancements to alter the aircraft's physical capabilities.

The 787-10 stretches the 787-9 to a fuselage length of 224 feet (68.3 meters), which equals an increase of 8.71%. Yet, Boeing certified it at the same maximum takeoff weight as the 787-9. FlightGlobal's 2018 technical analysis of the 787-10 described a trade that Boeing made on purpose. That figure is a key selling point, not a shortcoming, because the lower MTOW reduces airport charges and operating costs.

The wing of the -10 is shared with the -9 as well for economics.Boeing reinforced it in a few places, but it remained largely unchanged to keep production common. The result was a range of 6,330 nautical miles (11,730 km) for the -10, against 7,565 nautical miles (14,010 km) for the -9. FlightGlobal highlighted the shortfall against the Airbus A350-900, the -10's nearest rival in size, at roughly 2,000 nautical miles.

The Airbus A350 offered comparable capacity to the 787-10 but with vastly superior range, making it the preferred choice for premier global carriers. The larger seat capacity and lower range of the -10 is pitched as delivering the lowest cost per seat on routes where its siblings' extra reach would go unused. Yet, for airlines that only needed medium-haul capacity, Airbus also offered the cheaper A330neo, which kept Boeing fighting aggressively on pricing.

Today, the 787-10 sits in one of the most fiercely contested, complex 'sweet spots' in commercial aviation history. In terms of performance, its place on the flightline falls in a narrow niche that is wedged between the smaller 787-9, the longer-range A350-900, and the cheaper alternative, the A330neo. The -10 gives an airline roughly 40 more seats and 26% more cargo space than the -9. However, the range sacrifice is the clear trade-off. The Airbus comparisons are slightly more complex for prospective operators.

While they carry a similar passenger load, the A350-900 is structurally a much heavier and more capable machine. The A350-900 has a much larger wing and a significantly higher MTOW to carry more fuel, also out-flying the -10 but without cutting on seating capacity. The big Dreamliner has the cost advantage on shorter, medium-haul routes, but the moment a route hits 10+ hours, the -10 falls off the map, leaving the A350-900 dominant.

The A330-900neo represents Airbus's price-undercutting strategy against the 787 family. Rather than building an expensive all-composite jet from scratch, Airbus took the older aluminum A330 framework and slapped ultra-modern wings and engines on it. As a result, the A330neo is significantly cheaper to buy or lease, or around $35 to $40 million less than a -10. While the 787-10 is lighter and more fuel-efficient per seat on paper, its high acquisition price means an airline has to fly it constantly to make the math work.

A closer look at how Boeing is outdelivering Airbus in the widebody market.

For years, the Boeing 787-10 was a slow seller, but 2025 became a massive turning point. Boeing counted 150 orders for the -10 from just six airlines, an unprecedented year for the type. Key buyers included Qatar Airways, British Airways, Korean Air and Turkish Airlines. A full count showed 243 new Dreamliner orders by mid 2025, all for the -9 or -10 and none for the -8, which has drawn only eight orders since 2020.

BA led the way in May. Its parent, IAG, ordered 32 examples of the -10 powered by General Electric engines, with delivery between 2028 and 2033 and options on up to 10 more Dreamliners, according to MarketWatch. Korean followed in August with a commitment for 103 Boeing jets that included 25 of the largest Dreamliners. Turkish announced in September 2025 a firm order for 50 Dreamliners with options on 25 more.

By 2025, Airbus had effectively become a victim of its own success. The popular A350-900 and A350-1000 order books were completely packed, with delivery slots sold out deep into the 2030s. Airlines that wanted to expand their networks or replace aging planes last year faced a harsh reality: ordering from Airbus meant waiting nearly a decade for delivery. Last year's buying streak also shattered the historical norm of airlines staggering major widebody orders so they wouldn't all compete for factory slots at once.

Why does the largest Boeing jet ever made fly to Miami so much?

Recent orders from Delta Air Lines and United Airlines illustrate the 787-10 buying frenzy as definitive proof that the -10 went from a slow-selling niche airframe to a twin-aisle workhorse for future fleets. On January 13, Delta placed its first order for any variant in the Dreamliner family. After months of speculation that the deal was coming. DAL bought 30 examples of the -10, with options on 30 more, expected to fly transatlantic and South American routes when they arrive.

Delta has famously received all its new widebodies from Airbus for the past several years as it retires veteran Boeing jets like the 777 and 767. It had even famously canceled a legacy Northwest order for 787s back in 2016 after the merger. That made the order for Boeing's largest Dreamliner a notable break. Days later, United confirmed it had converted 56 of its -9 orders to the larger -10, with deliveries beginning in 2028, according to The Air Current.

United already operates all three Dreamliner variants, on the other hand, and has over 100 airframes in backlog. United's change was driven by takeoff and landing constraints at airports. US airlines face increasingly scarce gates and air traffic control capacity for departures across the nation. So the remedy for thinner schedules, without losing capacity, is to have each departure carry more people. The extra seats per departure with a -10 directly equals revenue.

When the world's most profitable legacy carriers make moves like this, it signals a paradigm shift. United is giving up range to squeeze more seats into its existing slots, while Delta is giving up the all-Airbus fleet model, ending a decade-long manufacturer boycott just to secure widebody delivery slots before the decade ends. Together, they prove that the -10's unique combination of high capacity and manufacturing agility is what the modern aviation industry demands.

An increased maximum takeoff weight allowance is key for Boeing.

While Boeing's reputation and cash flow have been heavily battered by crises with the 737 MAX and 777X, the 787 program has quietly kept the company's commercial division from collapse. With Boeing's massive flagship twin-jet, the 777X, crippled by rolling certification delays, the 787 family has become the default compromise plane. Meanwhile, the 737 MAX crashes, ensuing quality-control fallout, and regulatory delivery caps have severely limited cash flow from what was once the best-selling jetliner series in history.

The 787 program has consistently generated the revenue required to keep Boeing's books afloat. The sheer scale of the 787's success is best illustrated by looking at how its massive backlog shapes up. With between 1,000 and 1,200 unfulfilled backorders, the Dreamliner series fills 20% of Boeing's commercial aircraft order book. In response, the manufacturing program isn't just holding steady; it's ramping up capacity.

Found an error? Send it info@simpleflying.com so it can be corrected.

Following the consolidation of all 787 assembly line work away from Everett, Washington, the Boeing South Carolina plant in North Charleston became the exclusive home of the Dreamliner. To chip away at the massive backlog, Boeing has broken ground on a massive $1 billion factory expansion. It will add a 1.2-million-square-foot final assembly building, introducing a third line to the factory. The growth is actively paving the way to transition to a rate of 10 per month very soon.

The real reason you’re cleaning your oven wrong — and what the experts recommend instead

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Tackling baked-on oven grease is rarely anyone’s favorite chore, but the shortcuts you take to get the job done could be silently wrecking your cooker.

According to one appliance expert, several well-meaning cleaning methods can damage your appliance’s surface and reduce its performance.

While you might feel confident with how to clean your oven, Ecem Uslubaş, senior product manager for cooking at Hotpoint, says, “a lot of common habits are based on what’s always been done rather than what works for each surface.”

And she adds, “Getting it wrong doesn’t always cause obvious damage straight away, but it can impact the performance of your appliance without regular care.”

So, before you slip on your household gloves and give your oven a deep clean, consider these six common oven-cleaning mistakes — and the safer routines you should adopt instead.

Reaching for the same abrasive scourer for the cooktop, the oven interior and the exterior trim is one of the most common habits.

“Enamel, glass and stainless steel all respond differently to abrasive materials," explains Uslubaş. “A sponge tough enough for baked-on food can leave fine scratches on glass or dull the finish on stainless steel over time.”

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

Instead of using the same cleaning sponge for all surfaces, she suggests keeping a softer cloth aside for delicate surfaces and reserving anything abrasive for tougher, more durable areas, as it will help protect each surface for longer.

Wiping a spill away once the cooker has cooled is far easier than dealing with it after several more cooking cycles.

“Each time a spill gets reheated, it bonds more firmly to the surface,” says Uslubaş. “What would have taken a quick wipe on day one can end up needing a specialist product and more effort a few weeks later.”

Even though it can seem easy to close the oven door and pretend the mess doesn’t exist, it will only prolong and worsen the cleanup. Instead of a simple wipe-down, your oven will need a deep clean if spills are left to solidify and become baked-in.

Aiming a spray bottle straight at the controls can allow liquid to seep behind buttons or dials, which may affect how they function. However, there’s a simple way to change this habit, protecting your appliance while still getting it clean.

“It’s worth applying the product to a cloth first, rather than spraying it directly onto the panel,” advises Uslubaş.

Shelves, trays and other removable parts vary between cooker models, and not all of them are suited to a dishwasher cycle.

It's also worth being aware that some finishes can be affected by the heat or the detergents used in a dishwasher, which makes them unsuitable, while some parts may only be suitable for cleaning on the top rack.

Uslubaş says, "Make sure you check the appliance instructions before removing or washing any parts of your appliance. It’s also worth checking before using any specialist cleaning product too.”

Lining the bottom of the oven with foil might seem like a simple way to catch spills, but it can interfere with airflow and heat distribution. Although it's easier to remove and replace soiled foil, it has a negative impact on the performance of your oven.

"Ovens are designed with specific airflow in mind, and foil can disrupt that, leading to uneven cooking results," explains Uslubaş.

As an alternative she suggests using removable trays or liners designed for the purpose, as they are a safer way to catch drips without affecting performance.

This Whirlpool electric range features two ovens and has a combined capacity of 6.7 cubic feet, with plenty of space to cook multiple foods at different temperatures. Plus, with 5 burners and Aqua-Lift self-cleaning technology, it's hard to beat.

The door seal is one of the most frequently missed areas during a routine clean, despite playing an important role in how efficiently the oven heats. I have to admit, that I also neglect cleaning my washing machine and dishwasher seal, just the same.

"Seals can gather grease and food debris just like any other part of the oven, and a damaged or dirty seal can let heat escape," says Uslubaş. “Wiping around the seal and door edges as part of a regular clean helps maintain both hygiene and cooking performance.”

Regularly cleaning the seal also lets you check for wear, such as cracks that can reduce the seal’s efficiency.

Cleaning an oven can be a real drag, especially after you've cooked a roast, as the entire appliance can be covered in grease. But, it can be made easier by cleaning it out once it's cooled down, rather than leaving until it's cooked a few more dinners. However, if you are in the market for one of the best ovens, Uslubaş says it’s worth looking for an appliance that helps clean itself, and suggests buying one with the following features:Pyrolytic cleaningThe oven heats up to an extreme temperature and carbonizes and incinerates grease, baked-on food and residue to a fine ash.

Steam cleaningRather than heating to extreme temperatures, a steam cleaning function involves pouring a small amount of water onto the oven floor and heating it to around 200-400°F. The steam created helps loosen any residue, which can then be wiped away with a cloth.

Catalytic linersThe oven’s interior walls are lined with a porous enamel embedded with an oxidation catalyst. The liner continuously absorbs grease and food splatters while you’re baking at normal temperatures.

Position change may await £5.5m Middlesbrough signing to improve chances of game time: Opinion

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Middlesbrough are flying high in the Championship this season. Boro currently sit top of the table and have bounced back well from their play-off final defeat last campaign.

Kim Hellberg’s side strengthened in key areas in the summer, and their squad has drastically improved despite losing key man Hayden Hackney. Their influx of talent has meant playing time has been hard for some, and none more so than Max Arfsten.

Middlesbrough signed the United States international from Columbus Crew for a reported fee of £5.5m. It has been a slow start to life at the Riverside for the 25-year-old, with just three starts this term. However, changing his position could help improve his minutes for Boro.

Arfsten is capable of playing anywhere on the left or right side of the pitch. Middlesbrough have seemingly brought the 25-year-old in to play at left-back, where he has featured 12 times this campaign.

However, the United States international was mainly used as a left-winger for former side Columbus Crew, and he excelled over three years there. Last term specifically, he created 25 chances across 17 games and had 44 successful dribbles.

Arfsten’s weaker traits come defensively, and being dribbled past 16 times is not a component of his game that Boro will want to inherit. His side also conceded 24 times whilst he was playing.

By utilising him further forward, Hellberg is more likely to witness his brilliance in the final third. His best display for the Teesside outfit this season came against QPR, where he featured as a left-wing back. He made three successful dribbles and created two chances.

Middlesbrough have to show some patience with the 25-year-old as he adapts to the Championship. Allowing him that freedom in attack will be crucial, and his 17 successful dribbles so far this term prove that.

Arfsten’s lack of playing time hasn’t been completely down to his performances. Middlesbrough have witnessed consistently great displays this season from Tommy Conway on the left wing and Neto Borges at left-back.

Even on the right side, Callum Brittain is irreplaceable on the right side of defence, and Amario Cozier-Duberry is proving to be one of the best wingers in the Championship. With his preference to play on the left, however, he has to compete with Borges and Conway.

The Boro attacker has netted four times this campaign. With one assist too and a successful cross rate of 35.7%, Arfsten has a tough task to get ahead of the Scotsman.

Patrick Battiston, the horrible fright

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The scene is undoubtedly part of the legend of French football. The name of Harald Schumacher is likely to send a shiver down the spine of all the supporters of the Blues who experienced the famous semi-final of the 1982 World Cup between France and West Germany, where the attack on Patrick Battiston by the German goalkeeper took place.

Unfortunately, and very sadly, this episode experienced a disastrous remake during a Spanish D2 match where Jesus de Miguel, the Tenerife striker, lost his life following violent contact with Jesus Owono, the Andorra goalkeeper, very far from his goal. In both cases, it is a goalkeeper who comes out in a reckless manner and hits the opposing attacker with his hip. Jesus de Miguel was hit in the chest, which caused a cardio-respiratory arrest. Battiston had been hit in the head.

Seeing him lying on the ground, lifeless, the Blues at the time feared for the life of their teammate. Meanwhile, Harald Schumacher remained on the sidelines, without showing the slightest consideration for his opponent, preparing to shoot from six meters: the referee did not even call a foul on this action!

Immediately, medical staff came to help Patrick Battiston. The Blues defender was then unconscious, the doctor will even have difficulty finding his pulse. He will end up being evacuated on a stretcher, with his captain Michel Platini holding his hand, his face defeated. Highly worried.

Taken to hospital, Patrick Battiston will have to remain under observation all night. The result, ultimately, was three broken teeth, head trauma and a fracture of the cervical vertebrae. A lesser evil, somewhere, especially since we now know that the worst was possible.

A few years later, Harald Schumacher made his mea culpa. “I regret everything that happened after the clash with Battiston. I already apologized for not checking on him when he was knocked out. But then I told myself that if I got closer things could get out of hand, explains the German goalkeeper. After the match, a journalist intercepted me to inform me that Battiston had lost two teeth. I was relieved because I was afraid it would be much more serious. »

Valve just changed its Steam release rules amid a flood of AI games

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Indie games are missing launch dates, sometimes by weeks, due to the unexpected delays.

Valve is updating its game release rules to try to unclog a backlog of approvals due to a recent flood of new game titles, many created with AI, the company announced. That follows news that indie games are missing launch dates, sometimes by weeks, due to the unexpected delays.

Valve's goal for new title reviews is three to five days, but it's currently taking up to 12 business days. The company instituted the new rules in part to avoid any re-reviews responsible for those longer times. "We've heard from a lot of developers and know how stressful it's been having to wait for review build updates longer than expected," Valve wrote in a community post. "More improvements are coming to the build review process, but these initial changes are a great starting point."

First and foremost, developers must run their games through Steam for at least five minutes to ensure it works on the platform. Valve notes that it reviews builds that have never been played through Steam, meaning that "even if a game runs smoothly when a dev launches their executable directly, they might have missed basic steps that would impact a user installing and playing via Steam."

Valve is also requiring devs to confirm common redistributables like DirectX and Visual C++, check that achievements are ready, confirm that Steam Cloud save paths are configured correctly, and categorize screenshots as gameplay, menus, concept art or cinematics.

Left unsaid by Valve is that the store has been flooded by AI vibe-coded games with inadequate pre-release checks. "The time seems to have spiked around September/October. Presumably [due in part to]... the overwhelming amount of slop now flooding Steam," said developer 2 Left Thumbs on X. That data was confirmed by Reddit user u/ploz, who found that 49.9 percent of games released on Steam in October disclosed AI-generated content. In June, nearly 20 percent of Next Fest game demos disclosed the use of AI.

The delays are having consequences for developers. "Our game's trailer amassed more than 60,000 views on YouTube, and we had more than 6,000 wishlists," Cubic Earth developer structmira told PC Gamer. "Now, our Steam Community page is rapidly filling with player complaints, and we've lost all momentum.

Man City could face further charges as Premier League confronted with ‘dilemma’

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Some executives are pushing for Man City to be investigated for the seasons since 2018 after they were found guilty of 114 charges of breaches of financial rules

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

An increasing number of English football executives are pushing for the Premier League to investigate Manchester City for the seasons since 2018 or bring further charges, although it is a subject causing significant debate and viewed as a potential “dilemma” for the game.

The Abu Dhabi-owned club were last month found guilty of 114 charges of breaches of financial rules in a case already seen as one of the most controversial in football history, although they are currently in the process of appeal.

Amid a temporary lull in the story as the game waits for both the sanctions and the outcome of that appeal, many directors have been pointing to key details of the independent commission’s final verdict.

Since the document laid out how City’s sponsorship deals were inflated by disguised owner investment, with accompanying graphs, an obvious question being asked is whether that just stopped in 2018, despite commercial income accounted as staying at the same level.

As one source asked, “what changed overnight?”

In its decision, the Independent Commission found that Manchester City and its Abu Dhabi-based sponsors were ‘Related Parties’. If the relationships did not change, then that would form the basis for more charges in subsequent seasons given the club did not report this or have the Fair Market Values of the sponsorships assessed.

The ultimate reason for the initial investigation was a tranche of emails from football leaks that were first published by Der Spiegel in November 2018 and covered the period from 2009 to 2018.

While even those who want scrutiny on that period have concerns about another lengthy investigation, the wait for sanctions – expected by many sources to come this month – has sharpened focus on the period after 2018.

There is at least some possibility that the independent commission recommends titles of 2012, 2014 and 2018 are stripped. While this is currently viewed as unlikely, such an outcome would immediately raise more questions about the era of success that followed 2018.

City built on Pep Guardiola’s initial 2017-18 title winning season with the most intense period of glory the Premier League has seen, winning English football’s first four in a row to make it a total of six leagues in seven years alongside a first ever domestic treble and the 2022-23 treble that brought the Champions League.

Executives feel that questions about this have not been adequately addressed by the Premier League, although the competition’s policy and legal guidance has been to refuse to discuss the case until it is completely concluded.

The Premier League have been contacted for comment by The Independent.

There is nevertheless a sense among executives that commercial deals after 2018 will be viewed as having simply been made subject to the associated party rules, and were already assessed at fair market value.

That line of thinking has been met with some scepticism, given the nature of the guilty verdict.

The counter-argument is that associated party rules would be irrelevant here, since any investigation would be about disguised funding.

City are still strenuously insisting on their innocence, and remain bullish about an appeal.

The prospect of further investigation is also seen as representing a potential “dilemma” for the competition and its clubs. Some feel agitating executives are getting ahead of themselves, and that there should just be patience for the current case to conclude.

A concurrent view is that there aren’t the same clear grounds for another investigation without evidence like the leaked emails, and that it might even be unnecessary if the guilty verdict is upheld and the sanctions are appropriate, given what the final verdict already says.

The latter is taken to mean meaningful sporting sanctions and focus on the hierarchy responsible.

Others feel the idea of another investigation is better saved as a “negotiation tactic”, especially with how abrasive City have already been in their position.

As one source said of the guilty verdict prospectively being upheld, “when you’re seeking to agree on a potentially severe punishment, the Premier League can be shown to have given them a concession and spare them further”.

“It is pressure useful for the end game,” another added.

That could also be acutely relevant to the future of the club if this case does conclude with a forced sale under the Independent Football Regulator, or even a voluntary sale, which certain rival executives are mindful of. Any agreement not to investigate further would ensure that potential buyers are not put off by the possibility of further uncertainty and more sanctions.

Some fear that another investigation would take another half-decade or more, and that the Premier League would suffer further when many are keen to eventually draw a line under the case.

“I’m not Mo Salah” – Bradley Barcola speaks defiantly about his big-money Liverpool transfer

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Bradley Barcola has said that he doesn’t feel any pressure to try and emulate Mo Salah at Liverpool and is instead focusing on creating his own legacy at Anfield.

At the end of the summer, the Reds spent an initial £106m (potentially rising to £123m, via The Athletic) to sign the French winger from Paris Saint-Germain, with the 24-year-old effectively brought in as the right-sided replacement for the Egyptian King.

It took the new signing seven matches to get off the mark for LFC, with that famine turning to feast in the form of a hat-trick against LASK in mid-October, and he’s followed that up with assists in the wins over Brentford and Brighton (Transfermarkt).

In an interview with Sky Sports, Barcola defiantly insisted that he doesn’t feel a burden to emulate Salah or repay the substantial transfer fee paid for him, with his sole focus being to make his ‘own mark’ and let his performances do the talking.

The France winger said: “First of all, I wasn’t the one who decided the transfer fee, so that’s not been my problem.

“Regarding Mo Salah, he’s a legend here, a huge one. He’s done big things here and, for that, I have an enormous respect for him, but it’s not pressure for me at all.

“I’m not Mo Salah, so I’m going to try to contribute whatever I can to the club and make my own mark on its history. I don’t feel any pressure about that.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

Whoever was signed as the effective replacement for the legendary Egyptian was always going to face inevitable comparisons with our former no.11, but such were the standards set by Salah that striving to emulate him would be near-impossible.

Nobody is expecting Barcola to match the Trabzonspor man’s 257-goal tally for Liverpool over a nine-year period. As the Frenchman acknowledged, his job is simply to deliver the best that he can for the Reds.

There have been some moments to forget in his first few weeks on Merseyside, such as the missed sitter against Brentford last month, but he also conjured up a tremendous assist for Alexander Isak’s winning goal in the same match.

The 24-year-old might’ve drawn a blank against Arsenal on Sunday, but his direct running along the left flank was difficult for the Gunners to contain during the first half in particular, offering the kind of proactive threat that we love to see from wingers playing for LFC.

Salah was a unique figure at Liverpool, and Barcola doesn’t strictly have to emulate what the Egyptian has done in order to be regarded as a success at Anfield in time to come. We’ve seen glimpses of what he can offer to the Reds, and time is very much on his side to build upon that promising start.

Tony Mowbray’s ‘be careful’ warning falls on deaf ears as 23-year-old Stoke City man stars against Blackburn Rovers

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Blackburn Rovers fell to a second consecutive Championship defeat on Tuesday night as they lost 2-1 to Stoke City. The hosts were angered to see a late Andri Gudjohnsen equaliser ruled out for a push on a Potters defender, but they might not have been chasing the game had Tony Mowbray’s warning been heeded.

Speaking to the Lancashire Telegraph post-game, Mowbray revealed he warned his players about Jack McGlynn’s ‘wand of a left foot’. Yet, the American midfielder starred for the visitors and caught goalkeeper Balazs Toth out with a speculative near-post free-kick.

Mowbray said: “I’ve been watching all week, I’d said before the game McGlynn’s got a wand of a left foot, be careful. From where I was sitting, I could see he was going to only shoot but the goalie thought he was crossing. Crazy goal. A great bit of play from the individual, of course, but I don’t think they warranted it.”

Mowbray was right to warn his Blackburn Rovers players of McGlynn’s abilities. The midfielder has been in excellent form for Stoke City, and his midweek goal takes him to four goals and two assists in 10 Championship games.

It was only Saturday that McGlynn scored one and set up the other in a 2-0 triumph over Rovers’ Lancashire rivals Preston North End. He was the scourge of Mowbray’s side too in another influential display.

McGlynn’s goal was the standout moment, but he also created two chances and completed 40 of his passes. He was heavily involved in the Potters’ play in possession with the second-most touches of their players (65).

He chipped in with dirty work out of possession too though, making six defensive contributions including three tackles and one interception. McGlynn also won five of his 10 duels, coming out on top in the midfield battle with Kristi Montgomery and Sam Morsy (all stats via Fotmob).

McGlynn had impressed in the United States with Philadelphia Union and Houston Dynamo before joining Stoke City for a little over £4m. He’s quickly emerged as an influential player for Stoke City and Mowbray’s comments show he’s someone opposition managers are increasingly wary of.

His form to date more than justifies the fee paid for him, and may have some Championship rivals wishing they had pounced to bring him in themselves.

Blackburn Rovers have influential attacking midfielders, but Todd Cantwell has struggled for consistency in the past and Ryoya Morishita is out injured. Someone of McGlynn’s goalscoring threat and technical ability would not go amiss in the middle at Ewood Park, despite Montgomery’s growth and Morsy’s emergence as a prominent player since joining.

Invictus Gaming beat Top Esports 3-1 to reach first Worlds semi-final since 2019

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Invictus Gaming knocked out fellow LPL side Top Esports in the opening quarter-final of Worlds 2026 in Allen, Texas. The result guarantees China a semi-final place after the LPL's 0-4 start in the Swiss Stage.

Invictus Gaming are through to the last four of Worlds 2026. They beat fellow LPL side Top Esports in the opening match of the Knockout Stage. The series was played at the Credit Union of Texas Event Center in Allen, Texas, on 3 November (US time).

The win guarantees China a semi-final place. That matters after the LPL’s 0-4 start in the Swiss Stage. IG now meet either Gen.G or LYON. Gen.G beat IG 1-0 in the Swiss Stage and finished it with a perfect 3-0 record.

TES played the event without ZUIAN. On 6 October the club announced that Bai ‘369’ Jia-Hao would move into the starting line-up because of visa problems affecting ZUIAN.

The stand-in still made his mark. IG won Game 1 after building a gold lead of more than 10,000. TES levelled in Game 2 when 369, on Renekton, killed JiaQi and TheShy on his own late in the game. IG then took the next two games to close out the series.

Every Knockout Stage match is a single-elimination best-of-five played with Fearless Draft. The two sides knew each other well. They met twice in best-of-five series in September. IG won 3-2 in the LPL Split 3 playoffs on 5 September. TES won 3-1 in the LPL Regional Finals on 17 September.

The prize money gap is now fixed. Worlds 2026 has a total prize pool of US$5,000,000, with US$1,000,000 for the winner. Each losing semi-finalist receives US$400,000, so IG are guaranteed at least that sum.

IG’s run began with a mid-season rescue. The team started LPL Split 3 in the Nirvana Group. The club then asked TheShy and Rookie, who were both on a break, to come back. The pair helped the side qualify for Worlds. Both won the 2018 world title with IG.

TheShy said he had promised IG’s CEO he would help if asked. “I came back to keep my word,” he said after the series.

Reaching Full Retirement Age for Social Security in 2027? Here's Why You May Not Want to File Just Yet

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Taking benefits right away isn't your only option.

If you'll be turning 67 in 2027, you should know that you'll be hitting a big milestone in the context of Social Security. Age 67 is Social Security's full retirement age. That's when you can collect your monthly benefits based on your earnings history without a reduction.

The earliest age to claim Social Security is actually 62. But for each month you take benefits before reaching full retirement age, those monthly checks are reduced. So if you were born in 1960 or later, waiting until 67 to claim Social Security can be a smart move.

But that doesn't mean you should rush to claim Social Security next year if you'll be turning 67. You may find that waiting makes even more sense.

While full retirement age is when you get your Social Security checks without a reduction, delaying your claim past that point boosts your monthly benefits by 8% a year for life. And while this incentive does run out at age 70, if you hold off on filing for three years, you can boost your Social Security benefits by 24%.

Imagine your full retirement age benefit is $2,000 a month. Waiting until age 70 to file gives you $2,480 per month instead. And that's not the only perk.

Each year, Social Security benefits are eligible for a cost-of-living adjustment, or COLA. If you boost your benefits by waiting past full retirement age to file, each COLA that comes through will be worth more money to you by virtue of having a larger monthly baseline check.

Also, if you're the higher earner in your household and your spouse outlives you, they'll typically be entitled to survivor benefits from Social Security after you pass away. The larger your benefits are, the more money your spouse stands to collect.

If 2027 is the year you'll reach full retirement age for Social Security, you may be inclined to take benefits at that time. But there's a lot to be gained by waiting longer to file.

Even if delaying your claim until age 70 isn't feasible, holding off for a single year results in an 8% boost. And a smaller increase could help your retirement finances, too.

Remember, the more Social Security pays you each month, the less pressure there is on your savings. Keep that and the points above in mind when your 67th birthday arrives so you don't make a filing decision you regret.

Fabián Ruiz scores twice as PSG hit Villarreal for six in record-quick start

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Paris Saint-Germain scored four goals faster than any side in Champions League history on their way to a 6-0 win at La Cerámica. The defeat leaves Iñigo Pérez taking the blame and Villarreal with one win from four league-phase games.

Fabián Ruiz scored twice as Paris Saint-Germain beat Villarreal 6-0 at the Estadio de la Cerámica on Tuesday 3 November 2026. The visitors led 4-0 at the break in round four of the Champions League league stage.

João Neves opened the scoring in the ninth minute. Désiré Doué added a second three minutes later from a Maghnes Akliouche pass. Senny Mayulu struck in the 13th minute. Ruiz then converted another Akliouche assist.

That fourth goal arrived after 16 minutes and two seconds, beIN SPORTS reported. No team had ever scored four times faster in the competition. By half-time PSG had eight shots on target to Villarreal’s one.

Ruiz added his second in the 50th minute, set up by Vitinha. Flashscore described it as only the second time he had scored twice in a game for PSG. He finished with a rating of 9.81.

David Boly completed the rout in the 78th minute, with Neves providing the assist. The 17-year-old was making his Champions League debut. He scored four minutes after coming off the bench.

The numbers matched the scoreline. PSG completed 677 accurate passes to Villarreal’s 237. Their expected goals reached 2.75 against 0.74. Péter Gulácsi made six saves to keep the margin at six.

Luis Enrique managed this without four regulars. Kvaratskhelia, Dembélé, Nuno Mendes and Marquinhos all missed the trip through injury. The win was PSG’s 10th in a row. It also took them to 14 European Cup matches unbeaten, equalling the longest such run by a French club. After four matchdays, they were the only club in the league phase on 12 points.

Iñigo Pérez left Alberto Moleiro and Georges Mikautadze on the bench. He chose a less attacking line-up than usual. By the sixth goal the stadium was half empty. The fans who stayed whistled the home side again.

Pérez blamed himself for the biggest mistakes. He said his side had got their idea of how to play PSG wrong. He added that they had the wrong mindset after conceding. He also defended Carlos Macià, who had only just turned 18 and faced Vitinha. Pérez put the youngster’s struggles down to his own tactical plan.

At his press conference he would not discuss his own future. He said the squad was devastated and needed to focus on finding solutions.

Villarreal’s European campaign has stalled. They lost 3-2 at Borussia Dortmund on 8 September. A 3-1 home win over Napoli followed on 13 October. They then lost 4-3 at Liverpool on 20 October. Domestically, their previous outing was a 1-0 defeat by Espanyol.

— Paris Saint-Germain (@PSG_English) November 3, 2026

Spain’s other clubs had contrasting nights. Barcelona beat Aston Villa 3-0 at home. Atlético de Madrid lost 2-0 at home to Bayern München.

Villarreal return to LaLiga at home to Getafe on Sunday 8 November. Their next Champions League match is away to Slavia Praha on Wednesday 25 November, kicking off at 18:45 CET.

Wolves can laugh to the bank after Leeds United transfer decision

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Wolves had a hectic summer as Cesar Peixoto surprisingly replaced Rob Edwards as head coach, although the major squad overhaul that many expected didn’t exactly happen.

Edwards had been keen to make major changes to the group following Wanderers’ embarrassing relegation, but those plans were ripped up after the ex-Gil Vicente chief arrived at Molineux.

Peixoto was willing to work with many of the current group, so Wolves opted to keep a large chunk of the players that had struggled in the top flight.

However, it’s worth noting that in some cases, the decision would’ve been made for the hierarchy, as offers just didn’t arrive. But, one player that did attract attention was Ladislav Krejčí, yet Wolves managed to retain the Czech captain.

The 27-year-old joined Wolves from Girona last summer on an initial loan that included an obligation to make the move permanent for around £26m.

So, it was a significant outlay, and whilst Krejci wasn’t exactly brilliant in the Premier League, he was one of few members of the Wolves squad that emerged with any credit from that forgettable campaign.

Football League World takes a look at the current betting odds at the top end of the Championship.

Therefore, there was an expectation that Krejci would leave this summer, particularly as he played a key role in helping his country to the World Cup as well.

Leeds were credited with an interest, but they ultimately signed Tarik Muharemovic as their left-footed centre-back, whilst PSV’s late offer worth up to £18m was understandably turned down.

The lack of serious bids for Krejci may have shocked figures at Wolves, because the former Sparta Prague man does have good pedigree, having done well in La Liga prior to his decent season in the Premier League.

Once Leeds decided to look elsewhere, it was always going to be difficult for clubs overseas to stump up the £26m that would have given Wolves their money back for the defender.

You can understand why PSV tried to test Wolves’ resolve with a late £18m offer, and there may have been a temptation to cash in considering those funds could have been used elsewhere.

But, it wouldn’t have been a good deal for Wolves, and even though Fosun have rightly come in for a lot of criticism, they deserve credit for sticking to their guns on Krejci’s valuation, especially as the player would have been keen to play at a higher level.

Krejci didn’t feature for Wolves for their first four league games as speculation continued over his future, but, once the window was shut, he was straight back into the XI at Birmingham - where he scored in a 2-2 draw.

Peixoto’s eagerness to rotate meant the defender, like everyone else, was in and out of the team over the next month or so, but it now appears he has earned his place in the best XI, having started three games on the bounce.

Unsurprisingly, that continuity has helped Krejci and Wolves, with the centre-back having put in back-to-back commanding displays against Watford and Cardiff, with the side recording consecutive clean sheets.

Krejci was particularly good against the Bluebirds, where he capped off his fine individual display with another goal, as he nodded in from a Kieran Trippier corner.

As well as his defensive qualities, Krejci’s ability on the ball is crucial to the way Peixoto wants to play, as he is capable of firing passes between the lines, as well as switching play.

With Krejci captain of his country, he also brings leadership qualities that Peixoto has highlighted, which is further proof that the boss is pleased with what he is bringing to the team right now.

Obviously, the major concern for Wolves is that January just brings about more uncertainty surrounding Krejci’s future, as his form won’t have gone unnoticed.

But, providing Wolves are in the hunt for promotion, which looks likely, then Fosun must once again insist that Krejci isn’t for sale unless stupid money is put on the table.

If he was to leave, he would need to be replaced, and the reality is that Wolves won’t be able to buy an upgrade on Krejci at this level, and the money he would bring in is nothing compared to the worth of promotion.

Plus, you would hope that Krejci acknowledges Wolves’ position, and the fact he has got his head down and concentrated on his football shows he isn’t desperate to leave.

Of course, if Wolves fail to win promotion, then the player would be sold, but there should be an understanding between Krejci and the club that they will revisit the situation in the summer.

Wolves will feel a return to the Premier League is a real possibility, and, if they do bounce back at the first attempt, Krejci is likely to play a big part, so there will be huge relief that the likes of Leeds and PSV didn’t make a stronger push for the defender in the last window.

Shopify Stock Fell After Its Report. Here's the Price I'd Pay for It.

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The e-commerce company's free cash flow climbed 61% last quarter, but the stock fell anyway. Is it cheap enough now?

Shopify (SHOP -8.74%) stock fell nearly 9% on Tuesday after the e-commerce software company posted its third-quarter results. At roughly $144 as I write, shares are about 20% below their Oct. 12 record close of $179.22.

The slide came even though revenue climbed 32% year over year to about $3.7 billion and free cash flow jumped 61% to $816 million.

Before the report, I wrote that I'd rather wait and see where shares settled afterward. The stock was around $157 then, or about 86 times its trailing free cash flow. Now the cash flow has come in slightly over the $800 million I'd penciled in for a strong quarter, and the price is lower. Is it finally time to buy?

Investors seemed to want more than a quarter that landed where management said it would. Shopify's guidance was for third-quarter revenue growth in the low 30s, and 32% delivered that.

But revenue had grown 34% in each of the two previous quarters, beating a low-thirties outlook for the first quarter and a high-twenties forecast for the second.

Gross merchandise volume (GMV, the value of orders placed on Shopify's platform) slowed, too. It rose 29% to $119 billion in the third quarter, after gaining 35% in the first quarter and 32% in the second. Currency explains most of that. In constant currency, GMV grew 30% in all three quarters. Gross profit, though, is where the slowdown shows. Its growth rate dipped to 28% in the third quarter, under the 32% and 31% rates in the first two quarters. And management sees high-twenties growth again in the fourth quarter.

Spending is also set to rise. Management guided fourth-quarter operating expenses to 29% to 30% of revenue. In the fourth quarter of 2025, they were about 29%. Chief financial officer Jeff Hoffmeister said on the call that the forecast reflects more marketing, including some efforts before Black Friday and Cyber Monday. A U.S. marketing test held down spending in the third quarter, and he said part of that spending will move to the fourth.

Taking the middle of each range in the guidance, I get fourth-quarter operating income growth in the high teens. That means profit growth might slow sharply from the 66% Shopify just reported.

I doubt any of these explains Tuesday's fall by itself. But together, they suggest profit growth may ease in the quarters ahead.

Shopify's free cash flow margin (the part of revenue left as cash after capital spending) was 22%, against 18% in the third quarter of 2025. This pushed trailing-12-month free cash flow to around $2.7 billion, up from nearly $2.4 billion through June.

Between the higher cash flow and the lower price, the stock's valuation looks much less stretched than it did last week. At a market cap of around $185 billion, Shopify now trades at about 70 times trailing free cash flow, against about 86 times free cash flow before the report and about 98 times free cash flow at the Oct. 12 record.

Still, not all of that improvement is likely to last. Hoffmeister said only about 1.5 percentage points of the margin gain came from lasting cost discipline. He called the rest more temporary, spread about evenly between the marketing test, the timing of some tax receivables, and an accounting change that shifted merchant cash advances (Shopify's financing for its sellers) out of operating cash flow. Management also expects the fourth-quarter free cash flow margin to roughly match last year's 19%.

Taking out the temporary piece, third-quarter free cash flow would have been closer to $725 million. That comes out to around 72 times trailing free cash flow, just slightly above 70.

Yes, the e-commerce company is still growing quickly and bringing in much more cash than it did a year ago. Shopify even spent $562 million of that cash to buy back its own shares in the third quarter.

But at around 70 times free cash flow, buyers are still paying for years of rapid growth. And they're paying it right as gross profit growth cools and management boosts its marketing.

Does the lower price make Shopify a buy? I think the drop moved the stock closer to a price I'd pay, but not all the way there. I'd start to consider shares somewhere around $115, or about 55 times trailing free cash flow.

That's about 20% under where the stock sits now, so I'm in no rush.

Daniel Sparks and his clients do not have positions in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Shopify. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.
