Trumper’s Remorse: Voters Look at the Last Two Years, Ask for a Redo

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

With crushing wins in the House and Senate, America wants to Replayola everything that happened since 2024.

Even the most forgiving retailers tend to draw a line on a return policy of 90 days. Show up after that, you’re out of luck. But voters across America on Tuesday took a look at what they purchased two Novembers ago and said: “How about two years; would you accept that? The merchandise is a little battered, but the tags are intact.”After months of political fighting both substantive and surreal, with debates over ideology and reflections on buttocks, American voters Tuesday delivered as strong a rebuff to a Republican president as has been delivered in four decades, turning the House of Representatives and Senate over to Democrats and essentially saying “you see if you can stop him.”Democrats won in swing states and they won in red states; they won in states where Trump had been ascendant and states where Republicans never gave up control. And they won, improbably, in rural counties all over the country.From Maine to Texas, Kansas to South Carolina — hell, as of this writing even Mississippi was in play — new Democratic Senators sprung up everywhere. They won so often cable news pundits ran out of water puns for their blue-wave metaphor.Democrats gained, as of this writing, a whopping 41 seats in the House, far more than the three they needed to win control. But it was the Senate — where a flip was far from assured and in fact unlikely just weeks ago — where the most drama abided.A need for four flips was filled, then it turned into five, then six. States where Democrats hadn’t won a Senate seat in 38 years (Texas) or in 94 (Kansas). States where Trump two years ago won by double digits (Ohio) and states where Strom Thurmond presided for half a century (South Carolina).And, maybe most unlikely, states where Democrats hadn’t beaten a Susan Collins in 30 years (Maine).As of this writing, Democrats had flipped seven Senate seats with an eye on an eighth in Alaska and a ninth in Mississippi. They were also holding on to all of the blue seats, including Michigan, called by CNN for Abdul El-Sayed at 2:30 am local time.There have been major Congressional shifts like his before, most notably in 1994 for Republicans and 2008 for Democrats. But both of those events occurred because of a galvanizing candidate in the winning party — Newt Gingrich in 1994 and Barack Obama in 2008. What was remarkable about this sweep is that it didn’t come from one central charismatic figure but a slew of grassroots personalities who understood their individual constituencies, state-by-state: James Talarico and Josh Turek and Annie Andrews and Adam Hamilton. (Also, speaking of Talarico and Hamilton, it’s nice Democrats finally figured out what the Republicans did decades ago: overtly Christian candidates play.)And, of course, the Trump remorse. Because there was a political figure who mobilized the electorate — just in the negative. How fittingly 2026 that the candidate who influenced voters most was the one people liked least, Donald Trump with his ICE and Iran and the fact that prices still seem to be going up while the only thing the president is bringing down is the East Wing.Perhaps the best modern comp to all this is 1986. That Election Day 40 years ago, Democrats also scored big in the midterms — also in the second term of a fading Republican icon, netting eight seats against Ronald Reagan.This election season has been one of the most entertaining, if also the most divisive, in recent memory as a showman president disliked even by his own party paved the way to a series of colorful showdowns.Books can be written about all the dynamics, but fights over Middle East policy roiled and at times threatened to break apart the Democratic party — right up until the final days with Haley Stevens’ shocking defection in Michigan. And yet somehow it all led to history — the first openly gay male senator in New Hampshire’s Chris Pappas, the first Black female governor in Keisha Lance Bottoms.Meanwhile, Republicans tried to look toward the future while grappling with the Donald Trump present, accepting his campaign bids even as they kept him out of nearly all of their ads. (Democrats, on the other hand, were all too happy to tether their opponents to the president.)And through it all the president himself, getting distracted with boasts and random talk as he hit the hustings on a campaign trail where he was only ambivalently wanted.The Democrats will face mountains ahead, not least thanks to the schisms in their own party; how will the Darializa Chevalier and Melat Kiros types in the House play with the mainstream types led by new speaker Hakeem Jeffries (the first Black Speaker in American history), and in the Senate how will Peggy Flanagan and Abdul El-Sayed meld with moderates like Turek and Hamilton, not to mention majority leader Chuck Schumer.The Democrats will also have to decide how much to lean into inquiries and investigations, as this is not what voters sent them to Washington for — it’s the economy, stupid, as Bill Clinton’s (and James Carville’s) words continue to redound.But the bigger questions lie with Republicans, who now must decide in the minority how to oppose Democrats — and how much to genuflect to a man they have spent much of the past decade paying fealty to, now newly irrelevant. Perhaps the person most confused today, strategically: JD Vance. What or whom exactly is he supposed to be defending, and how does he find a path to the 2028 primaries intact?All those questions await in January. On this November day Democrats really only care about one: “Would you like that as cash or credit?” Related Stories Midterms 2026 Kimmel Bashes Trump After Democrats' Midterms "Tsunami": "The Only Ass on His Mind Is the One That Just Got Handed to Him" Midterms 2026 Ben Shapiro Says 2026 Midterms Were "A Wipeout for Republicans": "The President Was Kind of on the Ballot"

This is part of The Hollywood Reporter’s ongoing midterm coverage. You can check out all of our pieces here.

Trump Crypto Empire Faces Fresh Heat: Justin Sun Seeks WLFI Sanctions

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Donald Trump crypto empire faces fresh pressure as Republicans lose control of Congress in the 2026 midterms, handing Democrats a sweeping victory. The political setback comes as Trump-linked tokens remain deeply underwater and Justin Sun escalates his legal fight against World Liberty Financial, the WLFI.

Sun is seeking nearly $264,000 in sanctions over alleged failures to disclose the project’s UAE ownership ties, and the midterm defeat gives Democrats greater power to investigate Trump’s administration and scrutinize potential conflicts of interest involving his family’s business interests.

Meanwhile, the market has delivered its own verdict. Official Trump, Melania Meme and WLFI tokens have fallen sharply from their peaks, leaving investors nursing substantial losses.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

The latest legal pressure comes from Justin Sun, who invested $45 million in WLFI tokens before his relationship with the Trump-backed crypto project deteriorated.

Sun sued World Liberty Financial in April, accusing the company of fraud and unlawfully freezing his tokens. The project later countersued, describing his claims as a “meritless PR stunt.”

Now, the Justin Sun dispute has escalated into a fight over WLFI’s corporate ownership disclosures. Sun alleges World Liberty initially identified Florida as its sole citizenship in federal court. Months later, the company amended its filing to identify 22 entities in its ownership chain, claiming citizenship across Florida, Delaware, Puerto Rico, and the United Arab Emirates.

Last night, my lawyers moved to sanction World Liberty Financial @worldlibertyfi for failing to disclose, until months into my litigation against the company, that it is a citizen of the UAE 🇦🇪 . #WLFI #USD1 For those who are new to this dispute, I was one of World Liberty’s…

The amended disclosure came shortly after an August hearing addressing World Liberty’s attempt to move parts of the dispute into arbitration. Sun argues the company should have disclosed its ownership structure earlier, before his legal team spent months challenging arbitration. He claims the late disclosure could undermine the federal court’s jurisdiction.

The WLFI Sanctions motion seeks nearly $264,000 in legal fees and costs from the company and its attorneys. World Liberty’s lawyers reportedly said they learned about the UAE citizenship after the original filing. The court has not determined whether the company intentionally concealed its ownership ties.

Sun plans to refile his lawsuit in California state court once the sanctions motion is resolved. Nevertheless, this controversy adds another complication for Trump Crypto ventures, whose foreign investment connections have already attracted political scrutiny.

The legal dispute arrives as Trump-linked digital assets remain far below their launch-era highs. The official Trump crypto token reached $73.43 in January 2025 before falling to around $1.50. That leaves TRUMP down 98% from its peak.

Melania Meme, the first lady of America-tied meme coin, has suffered an even steeper decline. The token previously reached approximately $13.05 but has since traded near seven cents, representing a loss exceeding 99%.

WLFI has also retreated from its all-time high near $0.33 to roughly five cents. Its fully diluted valuation remains substantial, but its market price reflects a sharp reversal from launch expectations.

The downturn has left Trump crypto investors facing losses while the project’s political and legal connections draw renewed attention.

World Liberty also operates USD1, its dollar-pegged stablecoin, which has grown into a major part of the project’s business. The company’s financial and ownership structure could receive greater scrutiny as Democrats prepare to take control of Congress.

The midterm results have not triggered investigations into Trump crypto ventures, but Democrats will gain subpoena power and greater influence over congressional oversight once the new Congress convenes.

The Trump crypto story is no longer just about political branding and token launches. It is increasingly about investor losses, corporate transparency, and the legal consequences of operating a business tied to the presidency.

Bitcoin bounces from 7-week low under $76K as Democrats sweep Congress

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin price downside reached a seven-week-low of $75,618 as 24-hour crypto long liquidations neared $300 million.

Bitcoin (BTC) sought to erase 3% overnight losses on Wednesday as crypto markets digested the Democratic sweep of the midterm elections.

Data from TradingView showed BTC/USD falling into Tuesday’s daily close as election results came in, with Democrats going on to win control of both the House and Senate. The pair dropped to a low of $75,618 on Bitstamp, a level last seen on Sept. 16, before rebounding as high as $78,039 over the next three hours.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Data from CoinGlass showed the price dip liquidating late BTC long positions below $76,000, while a wall of asks kept upside contained below $78,000. Total crypto long liquidations over the past 24 hours stood at $288 million.

BTC liquidation heatmap. Source: CoinGlass

In comments sent to Cointelegraph, Kelly Ye, head of research at institutional digital asset trading platform Talos, said that markets had largely priced in the election outcome.

“Bitcoin moved about 1.7% after the polls closed, close to the roughly 1.8% options had priced, and there was no major leverage flush. The election was the main event, but markets had largely prepared for it,” she said.

US stock futures showed similar moves, initially falling before recouping some losses. S&P 500 and Nasdaq 100 futures were both down by about 0.3% at the time of writing.

S&P 500 futures one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Late Tuesday, Anthony Saglimbene, chief market strategist at financial services company Ameriprise Financial, told CNBC that concerns over data-center and broader AI capital expenditure were producing an adverse market reaction to the Democratic gains.

“If you get some governorships that are Democratic, what does that mean for the data center buildout, which has already seen a lot of skepticism over the last couple weeks in terms of the hyperscaler capex spending?” he asked.

In an interview with The Wall Street Journal, US President Donald Trump, who previously said he could become “Mr. Veto” to block Democratic policy initiatives, struck a conciliatory tone.

“I think we’ll work with the Democrats, and we’ll get along very well with them,” he said.

Bitcoin’s drop to $75,618 coincided with its 50-week simple moving average (SMA), a trend line that has held as support since mid-September.

Related: Here’s what happened in crypto today

In an analysis posted on X, Keith Alan, co-founder of crypto trading suite Material Indicators, identified the 50-week SMA as a line in the sand for Bitcoin bulls. Failure to defend it, he argued, would open the door to a “deeper correction ahead.”

BTC/USD one-day chart with 50 SMA. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Crypto trader and analyst Rekt Capital, who had forecast BTC/USD dipping below $76,000 after a failed breakout attempt above $78,000, continued to flag overhead resistance on the three-day chart.

“If this continues then these newfound 3-Day Lower Highs may guide price back towards the ~$75.5k level below over time,” he said on Wednesday.

BTC/USD three-day chart. Source: Rekt Capital on X.com

Bruno Fernandes admits Michael Carrick was right to drop him in fiery interview

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

United skipper Fernandes was benched last month after criticising Carrick’s tactics but conceded the boss had been right in an interview following the remarkable 3-2 comeback win over Roma

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Bruno Fernandes denied that Manchester United’s remarkable comeback win against Roma saved Michael Carrick from the sack but admitted the manager was right to drop him for criticising his tactics last month.

The United captain scored their first goal with a twice-taken penalty as they came from 2-0 down to beat Roma 3-2 and record their first victory in 10 matches.

But after their previous Champions League game, a 4-1 thrashing by Como, Fernandes had blamed Carrick for United’s lack of pressing in a post-match interview – and the United manager responded by leaving him as an unused substitute for their next match, a 3-0 loss to Bournemouth.

And Fernandes addressed the issue in another fiery post-match interview following the Roma comeback.

Speaking to Amazon Prime Video, he said: “The manager took a decision of making myself be on the bench because I wasn’t performing, and that’s fair.

“Everyone felt that was a punishment for the words that I had said. And if it was, it was right. The manager is the one that makes the decisions. I have to accept them. I have to go forward with that.

“It doesn’t change the belief I have on the manager and on the staff and and every player that is in this club, and it doesn’t change my aim to win every f***ing game that I have ahead of me.”

Fernandes and Carrick subsequently held talks before the Portuguese was brought back into the team on Saturday against Chelsea.

And as he got increasingly passionate, Fernandes added: “It’s been tough. It’s been a lot of speculation in the media, a lot of lies around many things that have happened in this club between me and the manager, like that we had cleared the air then after when nothing of that happened.”

Carrick has been under pressure as United have only taken seven points in the Premier League this season and had lost their three previous matches in all competitions.

But Fernandes said: “We don’t need to save anyone’s job. But it was important. The club needed that win, the players needed that win. It’s something that we needed as a group of players together with the staff.”

From embarrassment to the Man United of old – Michael Carrick saves his job, for now...

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Man United 3-2 Roma: Carrick was on the brink of the sack at 2-0 down inside 20 minutes but United ended their nine-game winless streak in remarkable fashion

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Michael Carrick may still need a greater comeback than this if he is to taste another Champions League night at Old Trafford. But if this proves to be the last, what a way to go. The romantic in Carrick remembers what Manchester United used to be, the days that seemed to be consigned to the past. Suddenly, salvation came from the United of old.

When it seemed Carrick’s job was untenable, there was an extraordinary fightback, a victory plucked from the jaws of defeat, a strange kind of glory from complete ignominy. The crowd who had reacted to the award of a penalty by calling for the Glazer family to go ended up galvanised and roused, cheering a team who had been an absolute embarrassment for 45 minutes to a victory few saw coming, chorusing Carrick’s name.

He has a lifeline. “He needed that win tonight, the club needed that win, the players needed that win,” said the captain Bruno Fernandes. After 54 days without a victory, everyone did. “Big character, big fight,” said Youri Tielemans. “It’s unbelievable, really,” said Kobbie Mainoo.

United were both unbelievably and believably bad at the start. It defied credibility when they emerged as victors.

Carrick had said he knew it sounded crazy when he said he was looking forward to this game. By half-time, his optimism appeared delusional. But now and again, even when they are at their lowest ebb for decades, United can justify the faith.

Carrick had talked on Monday about a magical night against Roma. That was 20 seasons ago, when United destroyed them 7-1. Fast forward from 2007 to 2026 and, out of nowhere, this version of United stumbled across something wondrous of their own. “This place can do magical things,” said Carrick, attributing it to the power of Old Trafford. It was as good an explanation as any.

Roma could be forgiven for wondering what happened, if they are cursed on this ground. Two goals to the good, they almost made it three on the stroke of half-time. Gian Piero Gasperini’s side were showing it was possible to play dynamic attacking football and produce a coherent team in a 3-4-3 formation at Old Trafford, something Ruben Amorim never accomplished despite his best efforts.

Roma’s first win in England in 17 attempts and 25 years appeared a certainty. Instead, they departed defeated, taking regrets for eternity back to the eternal city. Overwhelmed by a Real Madrid fightback in the last 10 minutes two months ago, they suffered again in a strange sequel.

The catalysts for the comeback were Fernandes and Matheus Cunha. The Portuguese scored one goal and made another, showing why Carrick had to restore him to the team after his post-match criticisms of the manager in Italy. The Brazilian had a part in the first goal, scored the equaliser, then the winner and still had time to collect a red card.

Booked for celebrating his goal in the crowd, Cunha was sent off in injury time, sliding recklessly into Leonardo Balerdi. He is out of United’s trip to Lisbon to face Sporting in three weeks’ time.

Victory came at another cost: Carlos Baleba had appeared the first United player to sprint all game when he burst through on goal just after half-time but pulled up holding his hamstring and went off in tears. He presumably will not be seen again until December.

So he went off before the game was transformed. If Carrick has been luckless of late, United may have had twin strokes of fortune. They were awarded a penalty, with Gianluca Mancini deemed to have fouled Cunha, and then a retake after Roma’s third-choice goalkeeper Devis Vazquez saved from Fernandes but Bryan Cristante was punished for encroachment. The United captain’s aim was surer the second time around and he ended his own goal drought, which dated back almost two months.

There was almost an instant equaliser. Cunha released Bryan Mbeumo with a wonderful pass and the Cameroonian’s shot hit both posts but did not cross the line. As Old Trafford rediscovered energy and excitement, Diogo Dalot had a header cleared off the line by Cristante.

Then Cunha doubled his tally of goals for the season within the space of four minutes. When Fernandes crossed, Balerdi inexplicably missed his kick and Cunha slotted in. Shortly after, he whipped in a sumptuous shot off the near post to complete a remarkable turnaround.

“Matheus can do them things,” said Carrick, vaguely. The manager had played his part, too: bringing on Shea Lacey added a threat on the right flank, swapping Cunha and Mbeumo at half-time worked.

And so United’s nine-game run without a win was ended. A second victory in the Champions League this season reduces the chance they will fail to even get a top-24 finish. Yet when the Stretford End threw tennis balls on to the pitch in protest, it was tempting to think that, given United’s problems with football, they were merely seeing if they could fare better with something else.

To begin with, United had surged into a two-goal deficit, conceding twice in the space of eight minutes. It was the earliest any visiting side had ever scored twice at Old Trafford in Europe.

The lack of an alternative left-back has been damaging since Luke Shaw was injured. It was a factor in Roma’s swift double. Preferred to the dropped Lisandro Martinez, Ayden Heaven’s recall swiftly turned hellish. Dalot was dismal, even by his own standards

When Rodrigo Mora swept a cross-field ball and a jumping Heaven only succeeded in heading thin air, Dalot was caught upfield, allowing an unmarked Matias Soule to sweep in a low shot. Remarkably, Dalot was out of position again, allowing Soule room behind him, when the Argentinian’s shot hit Heaven’s arm.

Donyell Malen converted the penalty. It was his 12th goal in 13 games. There was almost a 13th, when Senne Lammens made a fine save after the Red Devils’ defence parted like the Red Sea. Forever unmarked, Soule then curled a shot just wide.

Roma could have finished United, and perhaps Carrick, off. Instead, he has a reprieve. “I don’t have a crystal ball to know what happens next,” he said. But whether with a crystal ball, a tennis ball or a football, this was hard to predict.

Democrats Have Retaken the House and Senate -- but There's an Even Bigger Winner Today

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Congressional Democrats aren’t the only one’s cheering.

Yesterday, tens of millions of Americans made their voices heard by casting or mailing in their ballots. These votes have charted a new course for America, with Democrats taking firm control of the House of Representatives and the Senate, according to the Associated Press. Starting in early January, President Donald Trump will be working with a Democrat-led Congress.

This congressional shake-up is a big win for Democrats. But at the end of the day, there's an even bigger winner: Wall Street's major stock indexes -- the Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI -0.38%), S&P 500 (^GSPC +0.36%), and Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC +0.83%) -- and investors.

President Trump will be working with a Democrat-led Congress in early January. Image source: Official White House Photo by Daniel Torok.

President Trump has enjoyed a unified Republican government in the first two years of both of his nonconsecutive terms. In each case, Congress passed, and he signed into law, a major tax and spending bill. The Tax Cuts and Jobs Act (TCJA) (signed December 2017) permanently lowered the peak marginal corporate income tax rate to 21%, fueling S&P 500 share buybacks, while the Big, Beautiful Bill (signed July 2025) made the personal income tax bracket reductions from the TCJA permanent.

A divided Congress likely takes major tax and spending legislation off the table, which isn't necessarily bad news for Wall Street.

But a Democrat-controlled Congress does have some sticking points.

DEMOCRATIC SWEEP? UBS SEES 50% ODDS. UBS assigns a 50% probability to Democrats winning both chambers, while prediction markets put Democratic control of the House at 94% and Senate at 77%. UBS warns a sweep could complicate Trump's fiscal agenda and 2027 debt-ceiling negotiations, while tariffs and AI investment remain largely insulated.

For instance, debt-ceiling negotiations become a lot trickier. The ideological differences between America's two parties increase the likelihood of a lengthy government shutdown that could harm U.S. gross domestic product and corporate earnings.

Democratic lawmakers also favor stricter artificial intelligence (AI) regulations. Whereas the president has minimized AI regulation, Democrats prefer limiting data center expansion and ramping up the testing and vetting of large language models.

There's definitely room for near-term uncertainty to creep into the picture ahead of January 2027.

But if there's one thing history has shown investors, it's that they're the biggest winners on Election Night. No matter how the political puzzle pieces are arranged, the Dow, S&P 500, Nasdaq Composite, and investors always come out as long-term beneficiaries.

Admittedly, certain political combinations work better than others. According to online retirement planning platform Retirement Researcher, the benchmark S&P 500 has, on average, gained 16.63% annually under a Democratic president and a divided Congress since 1926. By comparison, gains taper to an annual average of 7.33% with a Republican president and a split Congress.

However, a 7.33% average annual return still tops other popular asset classes, including Treasury bonds, commodities, and real estate. Even in less-than-ideal historical scenarios, the stock market delivers superior returns.

Furthermore, this data shows that while fiscal policy does play a role in corporate America, it's the operating results, management actions, and innovative prowess of public companies that ultimately push the Dow Jones Industrial Average, S&P 500, and Nasdaq Composite higher over the long term.

The AI infrastructure build-out, the evolution of quantum computing, significant S&P 500 share buybacks, and record-breaking initial public offerings won't stop just because the congressional puzzle pieces will be rearranged in January. With another election in the books, investors are yet again the big winners.

Michael Carrick and Matheus Cunha appeared to be involved in a disagreement shortly after Manchester United’s dramatic 3-2 win over AS Roma in the Champions League.

United completed a stunning second-half comeback against their Italian opponents at Old Trafford on Tuesday night, with Cunha playing a starring role.

The Red Devils’ miserable start to the 2026-27 season threatened to continue after conceding two goals inside the opening 17 minutes.

However, a re-taken Bruno Fernandes penalty in the 58th minute turned the game on its head.

Cunha secured Man Utd’s third victory of the campaign with two late goals in the space of three minutes, sending the home crowd wild.

But the Brazilian’s decision to celebrate his second goal by removing his shirt and jumping into the crowd backfired. Spanish referee Jesus Gil Manzano booked Cunha for the celebration, before showing him a second yellow card four minutes later for a late tackle.

Red-carded players are requested to leave the pitch and head back to the dressing room, but Cunha was seen hovering around the tunnel.

When Manzano blew the full-time whistle, Cunha returned to the pitch to celebrate with his teammates.

Manchester United recorded their first win in 10 matches with a much-needed 3-2 comeback success against Roma in the Champions League

TV cameras then spotted an eyebrow-raising incident between Carrick and Cunha, who was visibly unhappy after being instructed to leave the pitch alongside a member of staff, stopping before the tunnel to applaud the fans. Watch the footage below:

Per the Manchester Evening News, it’s understood that Carrick told Cunha to return to the dressing room in order to avoid being hit with another punishment following his dismissal.

However, the South American now faces further punishment after appearing to break the rules - specifically IFAB’s Law 12, covering fouls and misconduct, stating players who are sent off must leave the pitch and head to the dressing room or a spectator area.

An extended ban for Cunha is now a possibility if UEFA decide Cunha is guilty of breaching the laws of the game.

The Brazil international, 27, will already miss United’s next Champions League match against Sporting on November 25. He could also end up missing the clash against RB Leipzig on December 12 if he’s slapped with an additional one-match ban following Tuesday’s antics.

Moreover, Cunha's actions have reportedly left both himself and United at risk of a fine.

“I was a bit disappointed with how it finished [red card] but it just shows how much he cares and [what] he's willing to do when he's in that frame of mind,” Carrick said, per Fabrizio Romano.

Meanwhile, Cunha added: "What a magic day. Of course, apart from the red card.”

He continued: “The mentality was beautiful in the second half. Let it be a turning point in the season."

United’s crucial win over Roma leaves them 17th in the Champions League table, with six points registered from a possible 12.

Man Utd Agree Personal Terms with Ait-Nouri for January Move

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Manchester United have agreed personal terms with Rayan Ait-Nouri over a potential move to Old Trafford in January, according to Sky Sports Germany journalist Florian Plettenberg.

The Red Devils have been keen to bring in a left-back since the summer window, when a move for Lewis Hall couldn't be completed and the club instead opted against bringing in an alternative, despite the clear need for strengthening in the position.

Shaw has only managed to make five appearances so far this season due to different injury issues, and with Patrick Dorgu also suffering with injuries, plus Diogo Dalot and Noussair Mazraoui struggling for form, it seems as though the club are now looking at options to change the situation in January.

Another player that Man Utd chiefs looked at bringing in was Ait-Nouri on deadline day, with GIVEMESPORT exclusively reporting that contact had been made about a deal.

Both clubs were open to a move, but City wanted to include an obligation to buy clause in any deal, and an agreement couldn't be found. But United are now considering making a move again in the winter window, with the player keen to depart the Etihad Stadium for more regular first-team football.

According to Plettenberg, personal terms would not be an issue with any move, but the rivalry between the clubs remains a factor in talks. However, an exit is being explored by the player's representatives and Man City, with Enzo Maresca having him way down the pecking order.

The Italian manager replaced Pep Guardiola in the summer but has admitted that he views the Algerian as more of a winger than a left-back, which has severely affected his game time in this campaign.

"When he was at Wolves, I know he was playing back five, so it was more wing-back than full-back," Maresca explained.

"In this moment, I see him more winger than full-back. But that means that in the future, if we need him as a full-back, we are not going to use him? Probably yes (we will use him there), but in this moment, yeah, he's more a winger."

So far this season, the former Wolves star has managed just one start in all competitions, and is yet to feature for a single minute in the Premier League. He has registered three assists in four appearances, however, showing that he can still contribute if given the chance.

When Man Utd appointed Ruben Amorim as manager in 2025, Ait-Nouri was one of the players linked with a move to improve the wing-back position for the Portuguese manager's system.

The club opted for Patrick Dorgu instead, and Ait-Nouri went on to join City the following summer. But the move hasn't really worked out for the 25-year-old, and an exit now looks to be the best solution for all involved.

United are in desperate need of a left-back, and bringing in a player with Champions League and Premier League experience would be a huge help to Michael Carrick, or whoever the manager may be at the time. Dealing with Man City is not something that is done often or easily, but this is a deal that makes sense for everyone, and United would be the ones getting the upside.

Whether he should be the first-choice target is a different matter, and there is no suggestion that he is at this stage, but if a deal is there to be done then INEOS would be crazy not to look into it properly.

Club chiefs face contract crunches and a fragile defence while dreaming of a transformative midfield talisman.

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Arsenal are in Champions League action this evening, away to Slavia Prague. There was a big boost ahead of the game with the return of William Saliba to full training, and the Frenchman is with the squad for the game this evening. Whether he starts or not is another question, but he’s clearly in good enough shape to be in contention for some minutes, and that can only be a good thing.

It was obviously a topic of conversation at the manager’s pre-game press conference yesterday evening, and provided an update on how the recovery has gone:

He’s in a really good place. I think the process and the timings and in the manner the injury has been dealt with, and especially the feelings that Willy has been having along the way, they’ve been very, very positive. So, his evolution has been as expected, which is not always the case. He feels very comfortable. He played 45 minutes behind closed doors last week and he feels ready to go. So whether we use him or not now is a matter of what the team needs.

There have rightfully been plenty of plaudits for Ezri Konsa since his arrival, and the way he’s slotted into the team, and there was a question about how he solves the two into three ‘problem’ when choosing his central defenders going forward:

So far we haven’t had that luxury, I wouldn’t call it a problem! We can talk about problems that are very different to that one. The moment that they are fit and available, we’re going to have the opportunity to give players rest or to load the players and to manage the congestion of the players in a much better way than we’ve actually done.

That is most definitely the kind of problem any manager would like to have. There’s no need to rush Saliba back, we can be cautious with his reintegration, and then when we have the problem of deciding who plays in which game, it feels very much like the kind of bridge we can cross when we come to it. But it’s a very nice bridge.

Beyond that, I think there’s some real room for rotation this evening. Players like Ben White, Cristhian Mosquera and Piero Hincapie will be looking for minutes at the back, and if we don’t see either Declan Rice or Martin Odegaard in the starting line-up this evening, I’d be absolutely fine with that. The manager can choose two World Cup winners in midfield, and still leave out one of Myles Lewis-Skelly or Bruno Guimaraes if he does that – it’s the kind of ‘luxury’ he needs to lean into.

Speaking about Mikel Merino and Martin Zubimendi, he said of their involvement so far this season:

They are playing a pivotal role. If we focus on the players that are starting, obviously that’s something else. Mikel has started some games already, some important games and he’s coming from a World Cup, a very successful World Cup. We know the value of the players that we have in the squad. They need to feel important. I do think that they feel very important and we have to make sure that they continue like that because if they have that spirit and that belief within themselves every time they play, they will make things happen for the team to win the game.

Up front, it’s a little trickier. Eberechi was a doubt for the game against Liverpool, while Christos Tzolis player almost the full 90 after his return from injury. I wonder is there any scope to start Max Dowman on the right, which means you can time-share the left between the two I just mentioned and/or Noni Madueke. I think you also have to look at this as another chance for Viktor Gyokeres who did get on the scoresheet against Fleetwood, but on a night when any kind of confidence boost that might have provided was offset by the fact we lost to lower league opposition. His minutes have been a bit scarce so far this season, but you’d be very hard pressed to argue that when he has played he’s done enough to give the manager much to think about. He could really use goals and a decent performance this evening.

I suppose that defeat to Fleetwood might give Arteta some pause for thought when it comes to how much he might rotate, but if you can’t give players minutes in games like this, then they’re not going to be in the kind of shape you need them be in if and when they’re required for more challenging fixtures. It’s not to take anything for granted against a side who are top of their domestic league, but in Europe this season they’ve been a bit poor, and if this isn’t the time to test our squad depth, I don’t know what is. Let’s see!

Why Is SpaceX Stock Price Down Today?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Nidhi is a Certified Digital Marketing Executive and Passionate crypto Journalist covering the world of alternative currencies. She shares the latest and trending news on Cryptocurrency and Blockchain.

Nidhi is a Certified Digital Marketing Executive and Passionate crypto Journalist covering the world of alternative currencies. She shares the latest and trending news on Cryptocurrency and Blockchain.

Nidhi is a Certified Digital Marketing Executive and Passionate crypto Journalist covering the world of alternative currencies. She shares the latest and trending news on Cryptocurrency and Blockchain.

SpaceX stock price is facing selling pressure as more shares held by employees and early investors become available for trading. The decline comes despite the company’s strong third-quarter results, which showed a profit of $4.4 billion on $14.6 billion in revenue.

The main concern is the release of more than 1 billion previously restricted shares. These shares became eligible for sale on October 22 and were worth nearly $190 billion at that day’s price. SpaceX stock fell more than 9% that day, with nearly 200 million shares changing hands.

When SpaceX went public in June, most shares held by employees and early investors could not be sold immediately. The company introduced a schedule to gradually make these shares available.

The October release was the largest so far. Although shareholders are now allowed to sell, it does not mean they have sold all their shares.

Some insiders have already sold. SpaceX President Gwynne Shotwell sold approximately $52 million worth of shares on September 22 through a trading plan arranged in June.

December 8 is the next important date for investors. Depending on the conditions in the IPO prospectus, nearly 800 million additional shares could become available for trading.

Further releases are expected in March and May 2027, as well as after upcoming earnings reports. On June 12, 2027, Elon Musk could also become eligible to sell up to 6.4 billion shares, including options.

Analysts have an average price target of $257, while the highest target stands at $800. Bernstein raised its target to $268 on October 26.

However, SpaceX still faces valuation concerns. Its market value stood at approximately $1.8 trillion at Friday’s close, with the stock trading at nearly 50 times expected earnings for next year.

For now, investors are watching the December 8 share unlock to see whether early shareholders sell their holdings and whether the additional supply puts further pressure on SpaceX stock.

Australian Consumer Advocacy Group Blasts Ticketing Giants for ‘Fleecing Fans’

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Reps for Ticketmaster and Ticketek have responded.

Ticketek and Ticketmaster have collected Choice’s Shonky Awards, a dubious honor handed out to those businesses deemed to offer some of the nation’s worst products and services.

The ticketing giants were responsible for “fleecing fans,” remarks Choice CEO Monique Perry, as the consumer advocacy group on Wednesday (Nov. 4) announced its 2026 Shonkys. Related Kenny MacPherson, Veteran Music Publisher and Big Deal Music Co-Founder, Dies at 71 Jessica Lynch

The ticketing companies were fingered for “gouging consumers during a cost-of-living crisis,” reads a statement issued by Choice. “Both companies have used a number of sneaky tactics, like dynamic pricing or ‘in demand’ tickets, to push ticket prices for live events to ridiculous new heights.”

Time and time again, Perry continues, the ticketing companies have used “sneaky tactics such as dynamic pricing or higher-cost ‘in demand’ tickets, concealing ticket prices ahead of sale and strict time limits to take advantage of fans desperately wanting to secure a spot. We think that’s pretty shonky.”

In one example provided by the not-for-profit organization, John Farnham fans who logged on to Ticketek to secure tickets to a tribute concert were faced with inflated prices of up to A$1,650 ($1,153).

Reps for Ticketmaster and Ticketek have responded.

“Ticketmaster is committed to giving fans a safe, secure and transparent way to buy tickets to thousands of events each year. We continually invest in technology to protect fans, improve the ticket-buying experience, and make ticketing more transparent across our platform,” reads a statement issued to Billboard from the ticketing giant, part of Live Nation. Ticket prices are set by event organizers, not Ticketmaster, the message continues. “We do not use surge pricing or algorithms that automatically increase prices in response to real-time demand.”

And for “in demand tickets,” event organizers set the prices of hot tickets “closer to market value and may review these over the sales period. These prices do not automatically increase because more fans join a queue,” the Ticketmaster statement reads.

“We’re committed to helping fans understand how ticketing works and providing clear information about ticket pricing and their options.”

A spokesperson for Ticketek, part of the TEG, disputed the claims concerning the John Farnham tribute, noting the premium ticket, Ultimate, priced at A$1,650, was not affected by dynamic pricing. “Ultimate is a ring-fenced premium ticket and the pricing did not change for this category for this event,” reads a statement from Ticketek. “Ultimate tickets made up less than one per cent of the tickets available for the event.”

Where variable pricing was used for the event, billed as The Songs of John Farnham: A Living Legend – The Celebration Concert, fewer than 7% of tickets were affected, which meant that for most ticket categories, prices didn’t budge, according to reps. Where they did, around 3.7 per cent of tickets were in categories where the price increased, while 3.3% were in categories where the price decreased, while upwards of 92% of tickets were sold at their original, unchanged fixed price.

Ticketek reiterated that ticket prices are set by the artist, promoter or event organizer, and variable pricing is only used when they choose to — in which case, consumers are notified about potential price changes before they enter the on-sale. “The vast majority of tickets sold through Ticketek are checked out at a price set before the on-sale opens,” reads the statement.

Choice keeps close tabs on the ticketing industry. The group has campaigned for a cleaner, clearer secondary ticketing market and, as part of that crusade, bestowed a “Shonky” to Viagogo in 2017, citing “dodgy practices that tick off consumers.”

An independent, nonprofit member organization since 1959, Choice put a string of businesses and industries on blast this year, including Virgin Australia, Hisense, Petcover, Quest Protein Chips and Sony Interactive Entertainment.

The Shonkys are now in their 21st year. Read more here.

Nithya Raman & Karen Bass In Dead Heat As L.A. Mayor Vote Counting Continues In Nailbiter Election

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

After a campaign that got more personal at times than a Bravo reality series, no one Tuesday night has been elected Mayor of Los Angeles.

In a Democrat vs. Democrat race that saw old allies Councilmember Nithya Raman and incumbent Karen Bass face off for many of the same constituents, tonight’s results so far reflected the dead heat the candidates had been in for the past two weeks.

As of 10:48 p.m. PT, with nearly 500,000 votes counted, the L.A. County Register-Recorder/County Clerk’s Office had Bass with 50.7% of the tallied votes, and Raman 49.3%.

The 10,000-vote margin is so close that it will likely be a few days at least before a final count is recorded. Also, with half of the expected vote now accounted for, the initial votes cited Tuesday night are the mail-in ballots, so Raman could see a surge once the day-of votes start coming in as her core base of younger Angelenos tend to go to the polls on Election Day. Watch on Deadline

On Tuesday night, as Democrats across the state and the nation were swept into power on a so-called blue tsunami, KTLA anchors called the bid for the City of Angels’ top job “a wild ride.” Certainly from two-term District 4 representative Raman jumping into the mayoral fray at the last minute, and the incendiary primary campaign of The Hills alum / ex-Palisades resident / Donald Trump fan Spencer Pratt, this year’s mayor’s race has been remarkable. Add to that, the whims of mall builder and 2022 candidate Rick Caruso, Hollywood mergers and the decline of film and TV production and the endorsements of the Democratic elite, and the whole thing has been crazed — even by L.A. political standards.

Declaring herself “confident” of victory when all is said and counted, Bass had a watching event in Hollywood on Tuesday. Raman gathered with supporters at the Greek Theatre in Griffith Park as they awaited results. From a big Bass lead around the June primary to a double-digit lead by Raman a month ago, both candidates were fighting for Pratt voters to gain the electoral edge.

Tonight, Pratt mocked Bass and Raman for being “neck and neck” with an extra swipe at the incumbent for finding herself in such a tight position. “Man, now we won’t have a clear winner for like 3 weeks LOL”

Both Bass and Raman, the latter of whom is married to a TV writer-producer, have promised to improve conditions to make it easier and more affordable to film in L.A.. For all her history around the state’s film and TV tax incentives, the incumbent has been criticized for only truly turning the power of her office to stemming the sharp decline in production and industry jobs in the City of Angels as her reelection loomed. Raman has been accused of missing vital City Council votes on the issue.

This summer, as the antitrust lawsuit from state Attorney General’s office against Paramount’s $111 billion purchase of Warner Bros Discovery picked up steam, Bass joined Gov. Gavin Newsom and exhibitors in pushing AG Rob Bonta to strike a deal with David Ellison as the CEO was whispered to be threatening to leave California. The WGA-endorsed Raman was always opposed to the ParaBros deal, but once Ellison achieved his corporate dream with the sign-off by a federal judge, Raman used a Hollywood confab on October 1 to publicly offer a hand to the Oracle scion to work together.

Along with Pratt, both Bass and Raman back a bipartisan federal film and TV tax credit. With the Democrats’ big wins tonight, whether passing that program is a top priority or not remains to be seen.

Midterm Takeaways: Tech billionaires suffer horrible night as Democrats drub Republicans

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Democrats took control of Congress Tuesday night, delivering a stinging rebuke to President Donald Trump and his economic record as voters voiced deep frustration with rising prices, government corruption and the AI data center build-out.

The party scored an extraordinary pair of victories in traditionally Republican Texas. James Talarico defeated Ken Paxton, becoming the first Democrat elected to the U.S. Senate from the state in nearly four decades. Gina Hinojosa defeated three-term Gov. Greg Abbott, making her the first Democratic governor of Texas since Ann Richards left office in 1995. Texas became the clearest measure of how far the blue wave reached: Democrats swept the state's marquee races in a Republican stronghold long considered beyond their grasp.

The victories were part of a broader wave of Democratic gains. In South Carolina, Democrat Annie Andrews upset Darline Graham in a race Republicans had expected to win. In Ohio, Sherrod Brown defeated Republican Sen. Jon Husted. Democrats also flipped Senate seats in Iowa and North Carolina and won key races in Georgia and New Hampshire.

The results marked a striking collapse for Trump's candidates. Paxton, Graham and Husted were among the president's prominent picks who lost after he made dozens of endorsements.

The election also sharpened the political fight over data centers and artificial intelligence infrastructure. Texas is a major center of planned data-center construction, and Hinojosa campaigned for a pause on new projects until the state adopts tougher rules. Her victory gives Democrats more influence over Texas' approach to the build-out, though the state Legislature remains in Republican hands.

The results also delivered a blow to the tech billionaires and corporate interest groups that poured hundreds of millions of dollars into backing Republican candidates who might be friendly to the massive AI data center projects they hope to build around the country. Elon Musk, the world's richest man and the cycle's largest individual political donor, spent record sums in a failed effort to help Republicans retain power, including in Texas.

Musk's spending may have become a liability as voters raised concerns about government corruption and the growing influence of billionaires in exit polls. Talarico repeatedly attacked Musk on the campaign trail, accusing him of trying to buy "his very own personal United States Senator."

"Tonight's results are a wake-up call for corporate special interests and billionaires who have been rigging the economy," said Rep. Chris Pappas, who defeated former Republican Sen. John Sununu to win an open Senate seat in New Hampshire.

The outcome is poised to reshape the technology sector's relationship with Washington. A more adversarial Congress is likely to intensify scrutiny of tech companies and the AI data center build-out. Markets reacted as the scale of Democratic gains became clear: Dow futures fell and S&P 500 futures slipped Tuesday evening.

Economic frustration was also central to Democratic gains. Many voters blamed Trump's tariffs and the war with Iran for worsening their economic situation, while the cost of living remained a persistent strain.

Democratic control of both chambers gives the party a powerful check on Trump in the final two years of his term, including the ability to block parts of his agenda and intensify congressional oversight. The results sent a clear warning: promises to bring down costs did not overcome voters' frustration with prices, corruption and the country's direction.

Something potentially significant could happen for NEAR Protocol by early 2027. Microsoft Copilot AI predicts a highly speculative, unlikely scenario that could send NEAR soaring to new heights.

The concept involves a coordinated initiative in which a consortium of major technology companies and leading AI research institutions designates NEAR Protocol as the primary high-performance blockchain for a large-scale decentralized AI agent network and data marketplace.

In this framework, autonomous AI agents would utilize NEAR for coordination, payments, and scalable verifiable computation. This approach could lead to significant on-chain activity and establish NEAR as a crucial infrastructure component for the decentralized AI economy.

(SOURCE: Copilot AI Predicts a Huge Move for NEAR)

On the higher timeframes, NEAR has been consolidating in the $3.75–$4.00 range following a broader pullback from higher levels earlier in the cycle. Price remains above key intermediate support levels but faces resistance in the $5.00–$5.50 zone.

In a typical bull market, a sustained breakout above $6.00–$7.00 would confirm intermediate strength and open the door to higher-cycle targets, potentially leading to a long-term recovery toward the previous all-time high of around $20.

In an extreme scenario of decentralized AI agent and data marketplace adoption, breaking through significant resistance levels could trigger powerful measured-move extensions and multi-cycle Fibonacci projections based on the longer-term foundation.

Current momentum indicators are neutral, with considerable room for movement into the extreme overbought territory typically seen during parabolic advances.

Key near-term support levels are located around $4.20–$4.30 and $3.80–$4.00. Maintaining these levels would help preserve the broader recovery structure.

Several external developments could enhance this optimistic outlook. A lasting resolution to major geopolitical conflicts, such as the Russia-Ukraine war or a stable ceasefire involving Iran, along with normalized shipping through the Strait of Hormuz, could reduce risk premiums and encourage capital to flow into growth and technology assets.

From an economic perspective, if major central banks adopt more accommodative monetary policies while maintaining high liquidity, this would encourage risk-taking across markets.

In the cryptocurrency sector, continued growth in Bitcoin and Ethereum ETF inflows, increasing institutional adoption of smart contract platforms, rising interest in AI-related blockchain developments, and a sustained appetite for altcoins would create a favorable liquidity environment. This situation would particularly benefit high-performance Layer-1 networks like NEAR.

While Grok AI predicts a significant move for the NEAR crypto token, it would require something unprecedented. As a result, some traders are looking beyond established assets. However, investing in a presale often comes with a different, typically higher, level of risk.

LiquidChain (LIQUID) is a Layer 3 infrastructure project that describes itself as “The Cross-Chain Liquidity Layer.” Its goal is to combine the liquidity of Bitcoin, Ethereum, and Solana into one execution environment, offering a “deploy once, access all” approach for developers.

The project’s stated features include a Unified Liquidity Layer, Single-Step Execution, Verifiable Settlement, and Deploy-Once Architecture. Currently, the presale price is $0.014971, and it has raised over $988,000, just shy of its $1 million target.

LiquidChain also offers a high staking Annual Percentage Yield (APY) of over 1,100%. While the concept is ambitious, the real test will be execution. Interested readers are encouraged to research LiquidChain further and review the project details before making any investment decisions.

Under this highly speculative scenario, and assuming a full-blown crypto bull market returns alongside supportive external conditions, NEAR could reach between $45 and $80, with a stretch target toward $120+ by January 1, 2027.

This remains pure speculation and entertainment, not a realistic base-case outlook. The core catalyst would require extraordinary coordination among major technology and AI players that is currently highly unlikely.

Scott Parker’s Promotion Blueprint Offers West Ham A Way Forward

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

With Scott Parker set to become West Ham United’s new head coach, I’ve been looking back at his three Championship promotion campaigns to get a better idea of what supporters can expect. Rather than simply guessing how he’ll set up the Hammers, I wanted to examine the formations and players he actually used at Burnley, Bournemouth and Fulham.

And having looked through the evidence, I’m increasingly convinced that Parker will favour a 4-2-3-1 at West Ham. It was his preferred system during Burnley’s remarkable 2024/25 promotion campaign, and he also used it at both Bournemouth and Fulham, although he was prepared to switch formations when necessary.

As discussed earlier today, his record of securing promotion with three different clubs was always likely to appeal to West Ham’s decision-makers. But it’s the way he achieved those promotions that interests me, particularly given the problems we’ve witnessed under Nuno Espírito Santo.

Parker’s record is impressive enough without dressing it up. He took Fulham up through the play-offs in 2020, secured automatic promotion with Bournemouth in 2022 and then guided Burnley back to the Premier League with 100 points in 2025.

What I found particularly interesting was that all three sides used a 4-2-3-1 in their final promotion-season matches. That doesn’t mean Parker played the formation every week, but it does provide a useful starting point when trying to establish his tactical preferences.

Those figures show the goals conceded across each 46-match league campaign, rather than just the final matches. And the progression is interesting, with Parker’s most recent promotion-winning team producing by far his strongest defensive record.

However, there’s an important distinction between his Bournemouth and Burnley sides. According to the Premier League’s tactical analysis, Bournemouth actually used 4-3-3 on 27 occasions during their promotion campaign, compared with 13 starts in 4-2-3-1. Parker increasingly favoured the latter towards the end of the season.

Burnley, on the other hand, were much more closely associated with 4-2-3-1. The Premier League’s examination of their promotion campaign identified it as Parker’s first-choice formation, with two midfielders providing defensive protection and the number ten contributing heavily to the press.

To give you lovely C&H readers a clearer picture, I’ve taken the starting elevens from Fulham’s 2020 play-off final victory against Brentford and Burnley’s final Championship fixture against Millwall in 2025.

Both were recorded as 4-2-3-1, although the responsibilities of individual players were not identical.

The obvious similarity is the presence of two central midfielders behind three more advanced players. That’s the aspect of the formation I find particularly appealing for West Ham.

I’ve already seen plenty of people complaining about Parker’s supposedly negative football, but I find that criticism slightly strange when you look at what he achieved at Burnley.

Sixteen goals conceded in 46 Championship games is incredible. Make no mistake about it. His team also recorded 30 clean sheets, a remarkable achievement which helped Burnley finish the season with 100 points.

Burnley scored 69 goals that season, compared with Leeds United’s 95. So there is a legitimate discussion to be had about whether Parker’s football can sometimes be too cautious, but I certainly wouldn’t dismiss the defensive record as a negative.

In fact, I would expect the first priority of any manager arriving at West Ham to be fixing the defence. It’s crap.

There’s a certain irony here, of course. West Ham had the most porous defence in the Premier League when Nuno Espírito Santo arrived last season, and we all remember how Graham Potter’s team seemingly couldn’t defend a corner. Improving that situation was supposed to be one of Nuno’s principal jobs.

Yet in the end, we were left with a pragmatic defensive manager who had seemingly forgotten how to park the bus.

As Nuno’s dismissal following the defeat against West Bromwich Albion demonstrated, the club eventually ran out of patience with performances that were neither defensively convincing nor particularly effective going forward.

I think Parker’s defensive organisation is precisely what West Ham should be looking for now. And if that improved structure can give our more creative players a little extra freedom, then so much the better.

Nuno was brought in to fix Potters awful defence

Looking at Parker’s previous teams, I believe 4-2-3-1 would be a sensible fit for the players he is inheriting at West Ham. The formation provides two central midfielders who can protect the defence, which should immediately help address the problems we’ve had with opponents dominating the middle of the pitch.

But the benefit isn’t purely defensive. Having that protection behind the attacking players should allow them to operate with considerably more freedom, rather than constantly worrying about what happens when possession is lost.

It also provides something I believe West Ham have desperately needed: a proper number ten.

And I don’t mean somebody like Mohamedou Kanté being shoehorned into a false-nine position he’s not equipped for. I mean a genuine playmaker with an eye for a pass, a stepover and a trick, somebody capable of unlocking a defence rather than simply occupying a position on the pitch.

That’s where Divine Mukasa comes into it for me, although I could also see Manor Solomon operating in that role.

I think either could benefit from having two midfielders behind them, particularly if Jarrod Bowen is given the freedom to get into more dangerous attacking positions.

Of course, there’s an important qualification here. Parker’s Burnley number ten was often expected to work extremely hard without the ball, pressing opponents and helping protect the midfield. So it would be wrong to assume he will automatically select a traditional playmaker simply because he uses 4-2-3-1.

Nevertheless, the formation offers that possibility, and I think it’s one West Ham’s attacking players would hopefully enjoy playing in.

There’s another reason I’m reluctant to accept the suggestion that Parker can only produce negative football.

His Bournemouth team were particularly effective at pressing opponents during their promotion season. The Premier League’s tactical analysis recorded them as the Championship’s most intense pressing side by passes per defensive action, with opponents averaging just 10.8 passes before Bournemouth intervened defensively.

That suggests a manager who is capable of setting up a team to win the ball back aggressively, rather than simply sitting deep and hoping to nick a goal.

Bournemouth also conceded only 39 league goals during that campaign, demonstrating that Parker was able to combine a relatively aggressive approach without sacrificing defensive organisation.

And that’s really what I’d like to see at West Ham.

I’m not expecting Parker to turn us into some sort of attacking footballing circus, nor do I think fans should judge him on whether every game produces three or four goals.

But I do expect a team that knows its jobs, defends properly, competes from the first whistle and provides enough attacking support for its better players to make a difference.

The evidence from his previous promotion campaigns suggests Parker is capable of delivering that sort of structure. Whether he can reproduce it at West Ham remains to be seen, but there are genuine reasons for optimism.

Three promotions with three different clubs, a remarkable defensive record at Burnley and a clear history of using systems that could suit West Ham’s attacking players.

For me, that’s a far more useful way of assessing Scott Parker than simply dismissing him as a negative manager before he’s even started.

Kris Gonzo (commonly known to the West Ham community as Gonzo) is the co-founder of Hammers Chat (established 2014) and the lead video content creator and columnist for Claret and Hugh.

With over a decade of professional sports media experience, he is a frequent West Ham United analyst and contributor for Sky Sports News, BBC Football Focus, and BBC Radio 5 Live. He previously served as an official video and content creator for West Ham United FC during the club's tenure at Upton Park and featured in the official documentary commemorating the historic final game at the Boleyn Ground.

A lifelong Hammer since the legendary 10-0 victory over Bury, when he isn't providing daily match analysis on the Hammers Chat YouTube Channel or broadcasting on X (@GONZObignose), he can be found walking his dog and restoring classic pinball machines.

Elden Ring: Path Of The Elden Lord Officially Announced

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Elden Ring fans have recently ventured back into the Lands Between to rediscover its secrets after the long-awaited release of Tarnished Edition, which finally arrived on Nintendo Switch 2 consoles on August 28, 2026. The release marked FromSoftware's acclaimed RPG's debut on Nintendo hardware, allowing players to experience its sprawling open world and punishing boss fights in a brand-new way.

Tarnished Edition also included the critically acclaimed Shadow of the Erdtree expansion and a trove of DLC, including two new starting classes, additional armor sets, and new customization options for Torrent. While the release gave Nintendo fans something to celebrate, longtime Elden Ring players on other platforms weren't entirely left out, either. The additional content was also made available through the Tarnished Pack, giving returning players another reason to start a fresh adventure.

Now, Bandai Namco has officially launched another exciting opportunity for fans to revisit the Lands Between in Path of the Elden Lord, available to check out now.

Bandai Namco has officially kicked off Path of the Elden Lord, the third of four community questlines designed to bring Elden Ring players back to the Lands Between. The new event began on November 2 and will run through November 28, 2026, giving players nearly a full month to contribute toward several ambitious objectives.

Unlike traditional quests, these challenges are community-wide, meaning players across all available platforms can work together to reach the required milestones. According to Bandai Namco's official announcement, the four objectives for Path of the Elden Lord are:

Successfully completing these challenges will unlock a special commemorative artwork created by artist Louis Gibiard, also known as vladinsk. Additionally, Bandai Namco has announced a special Twitch Chat Badge featuring Warrior Jar Alexander, designed by Carsondrewit. For those unfamiliar, Twitch Chat Badges are small icons displayed next to users' names in Twitch chats, allowing them to showcase special achievements or participation in events. Bandai Namco hasn't specified the exact requirements for claiming the badge.

Path of the Elden Lord is actually part of a much larger celebration that Bandai Namco originally announced on August 31, featuring four consecutive monthly community events:

Each questline features its own challenges, commemorative artwork, and exclusive Twitch Chat Badge. The final event, Path of Shadows, will focus on objectives associated with the Shadow of the Erdtree expansion, including collecting Scadutree Fragments and reaching the Abyssal Woods. Players can follow progress updates and challenge reminders through the official Elden Ring Discord server, which is also hosting a separate emote contest.

As for the larger franchise, Elden Ring: Tarnished Edition is now available on Nintendo Switch 2, bundling the original game with the Shadow of the Erdtree expansion. Meanwhile, the new Tarnished Pack DLC is available separately for $4.99 on PlayStation 4 and 5, Xbox One and Series X/S, and Steam.

Apple TV's 30-Episode Sci-Fi Masterpiece Deserves Kudos For Daring To Change The Books

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Readers cannot help but scoff when a streaming service announces a new book-to-screen adaptation of their beloved sci-fi literary series. It is also understandable where this sentiment comes from. More often than not, book adaptations, especially in the sci-fi genre, turn out to be inferior takes on their source material. Fortunately, an Apple TV masterpiece defies the norm.

Most book-to-screen adaptations that alter their source material's story end up leaving viewers divided. Apple TV's Foundation, in contrast, has been nothing short of impressive despite taking many creative liberties. Long before Apple TV's Foundation saw the light of day, many efforts were made to bring Isaac Asimov's stories to the screen.

Unfortunately, when none of those projects materialized, Isaac Asimov's Foundation was almost deemed "unfilmable." This is when Apple TV stepped in and delivered an adaptation of Foundation that almost seems nothing like the original books on the surface. Yet, despite completely changing its source material, the Apple TV sci-fi show is undeniably a masterpiece.

Isaac Asimov's Foundation series is driven by a heavy sociological angle. Owing to this, the books are less about individual characters and more about the behavior of large masses. His stories span across centuries, where the protagonists constantly change every 50 to 100 pages. From a literary standpoint, they are brilliant. But for a TV show, they are a little too internalized.

On-screen storytelling is significantly different because it requires individual-driven mythos. Especially in the streaming era, viewers need rootable long-term characters to remain invested. This is why Apple TV's Foundation completely ditches the books' approach and embraces more of the "Chosen One" trope to drive its story.

The Apple TV sci-fi show also introduces radically different narrative devices that have nothing to do with the books. For instance, Asimov does an incredible job of capturing the Galactic Empire’s fall by walking through everything from bureaucratic rot to the negative impact of rotating generic emperors. To capture the same decline, the show resorts to a completely different device.

It shows how Imperial cloning allows the same figure to rule for hundreds of years, leading to the same ideological stagnation captured in the books. This creative liberty in the TV series is genius because it ensures it retains a permanent roster of characters while still capturing the source material's essence.

After reading the books, some might still find it hard to accept Apple TV's Foundation as a good adaptation. And that is okay! Still, it can be perceived more as an ambitious alternate take on the original story instead of a literal translation.

When it comes to accurately adapting their source material, there are far better shows than Foundation out there. However, Foundation would have likely failed if it had closely adapted Isaac Asimov's books. The original book series consists of a series of novellas. Every few pages, the story takes a massive timeline leap, where old characters die while new ones take over.

For a reader, these shifting perspectives in the novel are easy to follow because they are connected through the Foundation's core concept of Psychohistory.

With on-screen adaptations, viewers not only invest themselves in ideas and themes but also in characters and the actors who play them. This is why even Apple TV's Silo had to mark Rebecca Ferguson's return in season 3 even though her character, Juliette, barely shows up in Hugh Howey's second book.

Asimov's books are also heavily driven by dialog, where conflicts are resolved through trade negotiations and long board meetings. A fantasy or sci-fi TV series cannot purely rely on discussions and descriptions to portray major galactic crises and has to visually externalize the friction, scale, and spectacle of these events.

Therefore, even though Foundation is not the best direct book adaptation, it is fair to conclude that an on-screen take on Asimov's books could not have been better than the Apple TV sci-fi series.

Isaac Asimov's novel series is brought to life in Foundation, a television adaptation of the epic sci-fi saga created for Apple TV+. The show follows a group of exiled humans that find themselves as the last chance to save the Galactic Empire, primarily from itself.

The Spotify AI remixing controversy is about more than just music

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The issue of AI-generated music on Spotify is causing controversy again, but this time with a twist. While the streaming service says it's cracking down on AI slop, it's also planning to let users generate their own AI remixes and covers (and to charge them for it).

The pricing and timeframe is not yet fully defined, but, according to the Financial times, Spotify aims to launch AI remixing by around February 2027 for a $4-$5 monthly surcharge on top of the price of a standard Premium plan. So far the reaction among both users and musicians has been tepid, but visual artists should also be watching what happens.

Spotify's proposal might seem reasonable. AI remixing will be an optional feature for users who want it, and artists can choose whether to opt in to license their work for it. For those who do, their fans get new ways to engage with their music, and they receive a new stream of royalties.

That sounds reassuring until you consider how platforms like Spotify work. If enough artists join, participation could quickly turn into an economic necessity. If remixable songs receive more engagement, appear in more playlists and generate more activity, non-remixable work could become harder to discover. There's also a concern that AI-generated remixes themselves could crowd out original music.

Beyond Spotify, It's also a test case for a question facing every creative sector. Should technology help audiences discover artistic work, or treat art as raw material for endless algorithmic recombination?

In Spotify's vision, a listener might turn a ballad into a dance track or make one artist appear to perform another artist's song. The value shifts from the original work to how much it’s ‘reimagined’.

On social media, TikTok and Instagram already allow users’ visual content to be ‘remixed’. Applying the same concept to art transforms it from something we experience into something we personalise. Think the artist chose the wrong pitch? Change it. Don’t like the ending of a movie? Generate a different one

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

On Spotify’s own community forum, one person describes this as like spitting in the face of artists. It trivialises their intent and judgment.

Art's power often comes from the fact that someone else made the decisions. The point of encountering another person's creativity is not that it reflects our own preferences, but that it shows us a perspective we didn't have before. When every creative decision becomes are our own choice, artistic intent becomes insignificant . That's bad not just for the artists, it also for the consumer's ability to critique art.

Use Google Flights to score cheaper holiday airfare

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Booking flights often feels like a game designed for you to lose, with fluctuating algorithms, sneaky add-on fees, and airline websites that push expensive fares by default. Google Flights levels the playing field, but only if you know where to look.

Once you're on the site, the real savings come down to three simple moves: browsing without a locked destination, reading the color-coded calendar, and flipping past curated recommendations to lock in the absolute lowest price.

If your destination is non-negotiable but your dates have a little wiggle room, never book off the first search result. Click directly onto the departure date field to open the full monthly calendar view.

Google automatically scans fares across several months and highlights the cheapest departure and return days in bright green. Shifting your flight forward or back by just 24 hours can routinely cut fares in half, particularly by avoiding popular Thursday and Sunday travel surges in favor of off-peak Tuesdays and Wednesdays.

When your flight list first populates, Google naturally sorts your options under a "Best departing flights" banner. While convenient, this list is a curated compromise: Google prioritizes shorter layovers and reputable mainline carriers over raw savings.

Look just above the top flight card for the Cheapest tab. Switching to this tab strips away those convenience filters, exposing rock-bottom fares from budget airlines, longer self-transfer layovers, and third-party booking agents that frequently undercut direct airline pricing.

Airfare prices fluctuate constantly based on seasonal demand, fuel hedging, and seat inventory. If you are planning a trip several months out, do not panic-buy early.

On your route’s search results page, toggle the Track prices switch on. Google will monitor fare movements in the background and ping your inbox the moment prices drop significantly, ensuring you buy at the bottom of the curve rather than during an artificial peak.
